Cryptography in Faraday: specifications, code and checks

Every cryptographic action in Faraday, 94 in all. The index, the actions and the list of libraries below are generated from Faraday's manifest, docs/crypto/actions.toml, at release 0.1.9 (SHA-256 6e58350a76accc388d88827d15090c56d40c8be5c8e7c4e4eb49201c97b721ec), and none of them is written by hand. Faraday's own checks hold the manifest to the code: a cryptographic function the manifest does not cover, a change to one since its entry was last reviewed, or a change to the cryptographic code since the last passing cross-check fails Faraday's checks. The page is generated again each time the website is published for a new release.

Faraday's approach

Faraday follows published specifications: BIPs, SLIPs, RFCs and conventions other wallets already use. Where a specification has an established implementation, Faraday uses it as a library instead of writing its own. A library is code others wrote, published as a Rust crate and pinned to one version; Faraday's own code calls it, checks what goes in and comes out, refuses what it cannot verify, and does every step the library does not. Faraday writes code of its own only where no established implementation exists or none fits its rules for keys, and says why. Where no specification exists, Faraday writes its own, labelled "Own specification, not yet reviewed outside Faraday" until someone outside reviews it. Each action is checked against its specification's published test vectors where there are any, and its results are compared with a second implementation: code others wrote where it exists, otherwise a second version Faraday's authors wrote, which is not an outside check. Actions with no second implementation are marked not cross-checked.

Each row below is one action. Specification is what it follows. Code is the libraries it uses and the crate of Faraday's code that calls them. Cross-checked with is what its results are compared with. The full rule is on the Standards page.

Key to the terms

Specification

  • Published78A specification others published.
  • Own specification20A specification Faraday's authors wrote.

Cross-checked with

  • Others' code57Compared with code others wrote.
  • Faraday's second version36Compared with a second implementation Faraday's authors wrote, in Python; not checked against an outside source.
  • Not cross-checked28Compared with no second implementation.

Read the words

SpecificationBIP 39PublishedCodeLibrary: unicode-normalization 0.1Library: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withpython-mnemonicOthers' code
Action

Reads seed words typed, pasted or scanned, and checks their count and checksum.

Specification

BIP 39, Mnemonic code for generating deterministic keys, Deployed

Code

Library: unicode-normalization 0.1.25, by the unicode-rs developers.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: The sentence is read in NFKD and split on Unicode whitespace; the first word not in the list is reported by position; a word typed by pinyin, zhuyin, kana or hangul is composed to the listed word.

Checked against

tools/vectors/bip39

Published, by Trezor (SatoshiLabs), python-mnemonic's vectors; bip32JP's Japanese vectors.

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/vectors.rs::trezor_vectors_all_languages

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/vectors.rs::japanese_vectors

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/vectors.rs::a_sentence_is_read_in_nfkd_whatever_form_it_is_typed_in

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/api.rs::bad_checksum_is_detected

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/api.rs::parse_locates_unknown_words

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/readings.rs::typing_a_reading_finds_the_character_it_names

self-test bip39_vector

self-test bip39_japanese_vector

fuzz bip39_words

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: python-mnemonic, by Trezor (SatoshiLabs).

Area bip39

Faraday's code faraday_bip::bip39

Compared with python-mnemonic (bip39/).

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, and a published vector's answer is required of the reference as well as of Faraday; python-mnemonic's own to_hd_master_key is checked against the seed's master key, as a second BIP-32 master.

Vectors The ten wordlists; every vector of tools/vectors/bip39/vectors.json in Faraday's languages; the Japanese vectors with non-ASCII passphrases.

Random cases Entropy to words and back in every language and length; seeds under random ASCII passphrases; a changed word, an unknown word, a word too many or too few; every valid last word.

Differences Both read a BIP-39 sentence in NFKD, so composed words and the Japanese vectors as published parse on both sides (owner, 2026-10-23).

A passphrase typed in Faraday is printable ASCII, at most 256 bytes; python-mnemonic takes any text. Faraday's vector tests and start-up self-test give a non-ASCII passphrase as its NFKD bytes, and the area checks that a typed one, or one past 256 bytes, is still refused.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 3000 cases

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

not stated by the library.

The word buffer and the indices are Secret values, zeroized when dropped; the Mnemonic wipes its indices when dropped.

Limits

Word counts of 12, 15, 18, 21 and 24 only.

A word not in the chosen list is refused and its position reported.

A checksum that fails is refused.

Faraday's own calls

Word entry by readings and kana: typed readings find the listed words. Neutral.

no decision

Make the seed

SpecificationBIP 39PublishedCodeLibrary: pbkdf2 0.13 (through core/faraday-crypto)Library: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withpython-mnemonicOthers' code
Action

Makes the 64-byte seed from words and a passphrase by PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512, 2048 rounds, and adds the key it gives to the session.

Specification

BIP 39, Mnemonic code for generating deterministic keys, Deployed

Code

Library: pbkdf2 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: The passphrase is checked to printable ASCII before the derivation; the sentence and salt are built in Secret buffers; a key already held is refused as a duplicate.

Checked against

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

not stated by the library.

The sentence, the salt and the seed are Secret values, zeroized when dropped; the key holds its words and passphrase in zeroizing buffers until it is removed.

Limits

A passphrase longer than 256 bytes, or with a byte outside 0x20 to 0x7E, is refused.

Nothing is added while a stick is attached.

Faraday's own calls

A passphrase is printable ASCII, at most 256 bytes. BIP 39 allows any NFKD text, so a wallet made elsewhere with another passphrase cannot be restored here; a letter outside the range typed into a passphrase field is refused. Conservative, F155.

Link to this action

Make the master key

SpecificationBIP 32PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: secp256k1 0.29Library: hmac 0.13 (through core/faraday-crypto)1 moreFaraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withbtclib-wallet's bip32Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Makes a master key and its fingerprint from a seed or an extended private key, by BIP 32.

Specification

BIP 32, Hierarchical Deterministic Wallets, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: An extended private key typed or scanned is decoded by Faraday's own strict rules (78 bytes, version, zero parent and child number at depth 0, zero pad byte, scalar in 1..n) before bitcoin sees it; the key is kept on a pinned page; the fingerprint and a 32-byte identity (SHA-256 of the public key and chain code) are worked out once.

Checked against

tools/vectors/bip32

Published, by BIP 32's authors (bitcoin/bips).

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bip32.rs::vectors_1_to_4_through_master_key

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bip32.rs::vector_5_invalid_keys_are_rejected

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bip32.rs::vector_master_keys_import_and_derive

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bip32.rs::a_child_key_is_not_a_master_key

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/accounts.rs::master_fingerprint

faraday/faraday-keys/tests/operations.rs::the_abandon_words_have_the_fingerprint_the_bips_publish

faraday/faraday-keys/tests/operations.rs::a_master_key_is_told_from_another_by_all_of_its_identity

self-test bip32_vector

fuzz xkey

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: btclib-wallet's bip32, by the btclib developers, on btclib's own curve.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: which versions a strict reader takes: BIP-32's xprv and tprv, xpub and tpub, from btclib's network table (btclib reads SLIP-132's too), in bip32_ref.py.

Area bip32

Faraday's code faraday_bip::keys (master, private derivation), faraday_bip::xkey (strict decoding), bitcoin's Xpub::derive_pub

Compared with btclib-wallet's bip32 over btclib and ecc (btclib/), on btclib's own curve with libsecp256k1 turned off, through bip32_ref.py; which versions a strict reader takes (BIP-32's four, from btclib's network table) is ours.

Checked first: The wheels are hashed against references.sha256 before any run; check_driver runs btclib against BIP-32's vectors 1 to 5 and the invalid keys of vector 5 before anything else.

Vectors BIP-32's vectors 1 to 5 (tools/vectors/bip32/), python-mnemonic's master keys.

Random cases Seeds of 16 to 64 bytes on every network, random paths with every index, public derivation against private, keys broken one rule at a time and the master-key import.

Differences None known.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 400 cases

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-keys/src/identity.rs · master_id

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

not stated by the library.

The private key and chain code are overwritten when the key is dropped and where the decoded copy stood; the key lives on a pinned page; copies bitcoin makes inside derivation are outside Faraday's reach (stated in the module).

Limits

Only a depth-0 key is a master key; a key at another depth is refused.

An extended key text that breaks any of BIP 32 test vector 5's rules is refused.

Faraday's own calls

An extended private key is decoded by Faraday's own strict reader because bitcoin accepts seven of the sixteen serialisations vector 5 rejects. Conservative.

no decision

Derive a child key

SpecificationBIP 32 · BIP 44 · BIP 49 · 2 morePublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: secp256k1 0.29Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withbtclib-wallet's bip32Others' code
Action

Derives a child key and the extended public key of an account, by BIP 32 and the purposes of BIP 44, 49, 84 and 86.

Specification

BIP 32, Hierarchical Deterministic Wallets, Deployed

BIP 44, Multi-Account Hierarchy for Deterministic Wallets, Deployed

BIP 49, Derivation scheme for P2WPKH-nested-in-P2SH based accounts, Deployed

BIP 84, Derivation scheme for P2WPKH based accounts, Deployed

BIP 86, Key Derivation for Single Key P2TR Outputs, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: The account path is purpose'/coin'/account' with the purpose of the script type; an account or index of 2^31 or more is refused; the key text is [fingerprint/path]xpub with h for hardened. Explore reads a path typed with h or ' for hardened, of at most 10 levels, each index under 2^31, and takes the script type from a hardened purpose of 44, 49, 84 or 86; the account of an address path is the path without its chain and index, and none for the multisig purposes 45 and 48.

Checked against

tools/vectors/bip32

Published, by BIP 32's authors (bitcoin/bips).

self-test bip32_vector

no fuzz target: Takes a parsed path and a held key; the extended key texts are fuzzed by master's xkey target

Cross-check

Hold a key in memory

SpecificationOwn specificationCodeLibrary: chacha20poly1305 0.11Library: subtle 2.6Library: secp256k1 0.29Faraday's code: core/faraday-cryptoCross-checked withNot cross-checked
Action

Holds a key in memory blinded and on pinned pages; the sealing under a session key built in faraday-crypto is reached by no shipped code.

Specification

Own specification (no decision)

Code

Library: chacha20poly1305 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: subtle 2.6.1, by the dalek-cryptography developers.

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Faraday's code: core/faraday-crypto: Each key's signing context is randomized from the session's randomness, again whenever it changes, each key's blind drawn by HKDF-Expand under a count so no two are the same; pinned pages hold a long-lived secret apart from other data and are asked to stay out of swap; words, passphrases and seeds are values wiped on drop. The in-memory sealing (`Sealed`, `SessionKey`: a counter nonce with a prefix derived from the key, refusing a key built without shell entropy and a key past 2^32 - 1 seals) is built and tested, but no shipped code builds a session key or seals a value.

Checked against

not checked against vectors

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/blinding.rs::a_key_joining_the_session_is_blinded_from_the_sessions_seed

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/blinding.rs::every_key_is_blinded_again_when_the_person_adds_randomness

core/faraday-crypto/src/sealed.rs::wrong_key_and_tampering_are_rejected

core/faraday-crypto/src/sealed.rs::nonces_are_unique_per_seal_and_per_key

core/faraday-crypto/src/sealed.rs::weak_keys_refuse_to_seal_until_rotated

core/faraday-crypto/src/sealed.rs::exhaustion_is_refused

core/faraday-crypto/tests/primitives.rs::pinning_a_value_wipes_where_it_came_from

core/faraday-crypto/tests/primitives.rs::secret_zeroizes_on_drop

no fuzz target: Takes values the program holds; no outside input is parsed

Cross-check

Own specification, not yet reviewed outside Faraday.

An outside review recorded in a decision ends it; a reproduction from the specification by an outside party is asked for in docs/AUDIT-SUBMISSIONS.md §10.

not cross-checked: Nothing outside the program reads these values; the sealing is checked by the program's own tests

0.1.9 · core/faraday-crypto/src/pinned.rs · Pinned::new

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Secret and Pinned comparison is constant time (subtle's ConstantTimeEq, documented in secret.rs and pinned.rs); sealing is the chacha20poly1305 crate's, not stated by the library.

A Secret and a Pinned page are zeroized on drop; nothing is sealed in memory, since no shipped code seals a value.

Limits

Nothing held in memory is sealed: Sealed and SessionKey are reached by no shipped code.

Pinning is a feature of the desktop app and of the stick and Pi images (faraday-crypto's pin-pages); a build without it holds the same value unpinned.

Faraday's own calls

The in-memory seal's nonce is a counter with a prefix derived from the key, because the crate has no random number generator; no shipped code reaches it, so no secret is sealed in memory today. Novel.

no decision

A key's signing context is blinded from the session's randomness. Neutral.

no decision

no fuzz target: Takes a held key and a reason; no outside input is parsed

Compared with others' code: python-codex32, by Ben Westgate.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: three rules BIP 93 states that python-codex32 does not enforce (a threshold of 0 or 2 to 9, the seed's lengths, the order shares are handed out), written in area_codex32.py from the BIP's text.

Area codex32

Faraday's code faraday_bip::codex32

Compared with python-codex32 (codex32/); the worksheet's rows written in the area from its polymod.

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, and a published vector's answer is required of the reference as well as of Faraday; three rules BIP 93 states that the reference does not enforce (a new secret's threshold, the master seed's sizes, the order shares are handed out) are written in the area from the BIP's text, and the worksheet's rows from BIP 93's polymod alone.

Vectors Every vector of tools/vectors/bip93/, the 55 invalid strings included; volvelle-website's worksheet cells (tools/vectors/codex32-volvelle/); the booklet's checksum, derivation, recovery, addition, translation and fusion tables (tools/vectors/codex32-booklet/); the Codex32QR profile's sets (tools/vectors/codex32qr/).

Random cases secrets, splits (the same randomness), recovery, derived shares, corrupted strings; fresh sets of every threshold up to 31 strings at 48, 74 and 127 characters, the checksum worksheets of their strings and of corrupted ones, checksums made on a sheet, translation and recovery symbols, translation worksheets.

Differences Codex32: Faraday recovers only from exactly k shares without the secret, and derives a share only from exactly k strings at an unused index; the reference also takes more strings or a used index.

The codex32 threshold and seed sizes, which the reference leaves to its caller, are written in the area from the BIP's own sentences.

Action

Shows an extended private key at a path below a held key.

Specification

BIP 32, Hierarchical Deterministic Wallets, Deployed

Code

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-keys: Answers only its own reason; the key is derived at the path and written in a fixed buffer. On Explore each eye makes what it shows once, at the press that opens it: the seed's forms, the root's and the account's extended private keys together, or an address's private key. The account is the path's account, or the path itself where it is in none. What is not on screen is dropped after every press and key on Explore, and by closing an eye, leaving Explore, the idle warning and a lock.

Read a WIF private key

SpecificationOwn specificationCodeLibrary: secp256k1 0.29Library: bitcoin 0.32Library: hmac 0.13 (through core/faraday-crypto)1 moreFaraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1Others' code
Action

Reads a single private key written as WIF, typed or scanned for a sweep, and works out the addresses it pays.

Specification

Own specification (F171)

Code

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: Reads base58check with the bitcoin crate and holds Bitcoin Core's DecodeSecret rules itself: a version of 0x80 or 0xef, 32 bytes of key then nothing or exactly 0x01, a scalar in 1..n; the key is held on a pinned page in a signing context blinded from the key and, in faraday-keys, again from the session's randomness. A key of the other network kind, or read while a stick is attached, is refused; the key pays P2PKH, and if compressed P2WPKH and P2SH-P2WPKH.

Checked against

tools/vectors/wif

Published, by Bitcoin Core's key_io_valid.json and key_io_invalid.json, by the Bitcoin Core developers.

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/wif.rs::cores_valid_private_keys_read_with_their_form_and_chain

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/wif.rs::nothing_else_in_cores_files_reads_as_a_private_key

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/wif.rs::a_wif_breaking_a_rule_core_holds_is_refused

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/wif.rs::the_generators_key_pays_its_published_addresses

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/sweep.rs::a_typed_key_shows_its_three_addresses_and_is_never_loaded

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/sweep.rs::a_key_of_the_other_network_and_text_that_is_no_key_are_refused

fuzz xkey

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: Bitcoin Core v31.1, by the Bitcoin Core developers, which signs from descriptors of the same WIF what Faraday reads from it.

Area sign

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::sign (every held key selected, deterministic Schnorr, both RFC 6979 nonce rules) and faraday_psbt::finalize

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1 on regtest (core_node.py): descriptorprocesspsbt signs from the held masters' keys, finalizepsbt finalizes and extracts, testmempoolaccept judges each finalized transaction over its funding on the node; BIP-340's reference (secp256k1lab) and Core's key.py for the deterministic nonces, over Core's script.py sighashes; python-ecdsa (the distribution's package) and btclib (btclib/, its own curve) as a second curve lineage, beside them: every ECDSA signature again under plain RFC 6979 or verified, every BIP-340 signature verified and made again; python-bip380 (miniscript/) reads the leaves. Faraday's refusals and the inputs a wallet's rule leaves unsigned are ours, in area_sign.py.

Checked first: Core is the pinned release, hashed against its pin before every run; the vendored files are hashed against references.sha256; a published vector's answer is required of Core as well as of Faraday; every ECDSA and BIP-340 signature Core makes is checked to verify under the hash the area computes before that hash is used for Faraday's deterministic nonces.

Vectors BIP-174's signer, finalizer and extractor vectors; the Core-built spends of tools/vectors/psbt/ (2-of-3 sortedmulti, the Liana wallet down both paths, a taproot tree by a leaf and by the key path, BIP 387's taproot multisig), signed by every subset of their keys; the finalizer vectors there (or_b's smaller signature, the taproot leaves that tie by count, the taproot multisig and MuSig2 spends).

Random cases PSBTs of one to four inputs, each spending a funding output on the node: P2PKH, P2SH-P2WPKH, P2WPKH, multi/sortedmulti in P2SH, P2WSH and P2SH-P2WSH, P2TR key path, P2TR script path (pk, pkh, timelocked keys, or_d, multi_a) with the key path beside it, miniscript in P2WSH and P2SH-P2WSH; held and foreign keys, cosigners' signatures already on (Core's), every standard sighash type, timelocks met and not; Core's signatures (ECDSA byte for byte, BIP-340 by verification and the deterministic nonce), each final scriptSig and witness and the extracted transaction Core's, the cleared fields, and every finalized transaction accepted by Core's mempool; an origin that derives another key refused; sweeps: a WIF key's P2PKH, P2WPKH and P2SH-P2WPKH outputs (P2PKH alone for an uncompressed key) spent beside another key's, signed by Core from the WIF and by faraday_psbt::sweep::sign, byte for byte, finalized and accepted by the mempool.

Differences PSBT signing: Faraday refuses to sign a SegWit v0 input with no previous transaction in a transaction of more than one input; Core signs it, and BIP-174's own signer vector is such a PSBT. The refusal is ours, checked and counted. Faraday holds a finalized input to the same rule and refuses the PSBT when such an input is final, except a taproot one, whose final witness is the coordinator's.

PSBT signing, wallets: Faraday signs an input spent from a script of several keys (a multi of two or more, a miniscript naming two or more, a taproot script path whose carried leaves name two or more with its internal key, or are not its whole tree) only for a registered wallet that holds one of its keys and whose script it is; Core signs any. The area refuses, by a rule of its own, where no wallet the request registers holds a held key and gives the input, and counts it only where Faraday's refusal is that the wallet is not registered. A miniscript or a taproot tree of one key is signed only through such a wallet, and is otherwise left unsigned while the rest is signed (F141); the area takes Core's signatures off it. Half the corpus's multi, miniscript and taproot-tree inputs come with their wallet registered, so their signatures are still compared. A registered tree's every leaf has its wait met, since Faraday refuses a path that can never be valid, which the area does not work out.

PSBT signing: Faraday refuses a PSBT any input of which asks for a NONE or SINGLE sighash, and one that none of the held keys can sign: dangers no hold accepts (F136). Core signs the first and answers the second with nothing signed. The area expects both refusals by a rule of its own, counting a held key as taking part as Faraday's inspection does (a miniscript or a taproot tree only through a wallet in use), checks that Faraday's refusal names the danger, and counts them.

Taproot pkh leaves: Faraday signs a taproot leaf that checks its key by hash but does not finalize it: miniscript 12's finalizer looks the key up among the ECDSA origins and panics, and would push 33 bytes where the leaf hashed 32. Core finalizes such a leaf; the area takes the signatures under it off before Core finalizes, and counts each input Core would otherwise have finalized another way.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 158 cases

0.1.9 · core/faraday-bip/src/wif.rs · SingleKey::from_payload

0.1.9 · core/faraday-bip/src/wif.rs · SingleKey::scripts

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

libsecp256k1 documents its key multiplication as constant time; the base58 decode is not stated to be.

The decoded buffer is zeroized before the reader returns; the key is held on a pinned page and erased when it is dropped, at Done, a lock or leaving the Sweep sheet.

Limits

A key is swept and never kept: no vault record, no key slot, no backup.

A WIF of the other network kind is refused.

Faraday's own calls

A WIF key is never loaded as a key or kept: it lives for one sweep. Conservative, F171.

Secret handling

Reveal::Words

Reveal::Passphrase

Reveal::CopyByHand

not stated by the library.

The lent text is borrowed from a zeroizing buffer held by the key; the guard holds no copy; the key wipes its words when it is removed.

Limits

Compare two keys' words

SpecificationBIP 39PublishedCodeLibrary: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Library: unicode-normalization 0.1 (through core/faraday-bip)Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-keysCross-checked withNot cross-checked
Action

Tells whether two keys, or a key and a copy of its words, have the same words, by a SHA-256 of their entropy, and orders the copy check's questions.

Specification

BIP 39, Mnemonic code for generating deterministic keys, Deployed

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: unicode-normalization 0.1.25, by the unicode-rs developers, through core/faraday-bip.

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-keys: The id is all 32 bytes of the SHA-256 of the words' entropy, worked out the first time it is asked and kept beside the key, so two seeds never pass for one; the same words with and without a passphrase share it. What is kept of a secret's fingerprint is keyed by SHA-256 of a tag and the secret, so the secret itself is kept nowhere. The copy check's questions are ordered from the clock and a SHA-256 of the word numbers.

Read a wallet export

SpecificationBIP 388 · RFC 8259PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1 (deriveaddressesOthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads another wallet's export (Coldcard JSON and multisig configuration files, Specter DIY's addwallet command) as a wallet and checks the first address it states; writes a native SegWit single-key wallet as Coldcard's file for Wasabi, and any wallet but a MuSig2 or silent payments one as Specter DIY's addwallet command.

Specification

BIP 388, Wallet Policies for Descriptor Wallets, Complete

RFC 8259, The JavaScript Object Notation (JSON) Data Interchange Format, Status not checked

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: JSON is read by Faraday's own reader (depth and size limited, an object that names a key twice refused); a Coldcard export's stated first address is derived and compared; a multisig file with more than fifteen cosigners is refused at the sixteenth. An account key alone becomes a wallet of the kind its path's purpose names (44 legacy, 49 nested SegWit, 84 native SegWit, 86 taproot), and a purpose of 45, 48 or 87 is a cosigner's key and no wallet on its own; a key of a test network is taken as testnet unless the session is already on a test network; the file for Wasabi is written only for a native SegWit single-key wallet whose key has an origin at exactly 84h/0h/0h or 84h/1h/0h. Specter DIY's addwallet command is read with its name before the last `&`, its descriptor's checksum checked where it has one (Specter DIY drops it unread), and keys none of which carries a derivation as the two chains; a single-key or multisig wallet is written without its keys' derivations and with no checksum, as Sparrow writes it, any other with them.

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Only public keys and public text are read; nothing here holds a secret.

Nothing secret is held.

Limits

A file whose stated first address is not its wallet's is refused.

A key in a SLIP-132 version that disagrees with the script is refused.

A receive and a change descriptor are one wallet only when they are the same but for /0/* and /1/*, each with its checksum, if any, holding; otherwise the file is refused.

A file for Wasabi is written only for a native SegWit single-key wallet at 84h/0h/0h (mainnet) or 84h/1h/0h (test networks).

Faraday's own calls

A SLIP-132 version is checked against the script and the path around the key. Conservative, F155.

A Caravan file whose startingAddressIndex is not 0, and an Electrum multisig keystore with no fingerprint, are refused. Conservative, F155.

Link to this action

Read BSMS records

SpecificationBIP 129 · BIP 137PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: secp256k1 0.29Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withbtclib's bms and btclib-wallet's BIP-32Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads BSMS key and descriptor records, checks the signature on a signer's record and writes this device's own signed record.

Specification

BIP 129, Bitcoin Secure Multisig Setup (BSMS), Complete, vendored

BIP 137, Signatures of Messages using Private Keys, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: The signature is the legacy compact recoverable signature every signer writes: the public key is recovered from it and compared with the record's; a descriptor record's first address is derived and compared; a record signed with a secret that is not the account's is refused rather than written.

Checked against

tools/vectors/bip129

Published, by BIP 129's authors (bitcoin/bips).

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::every_key_record_the_bip_writes_is_read_and_its_signature_holds

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::a_key_record_changed_after_signing_does_not_verify

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::every_descriptor_record_the_bip_writes_is_its_wallet_and_its_first_address

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::a_descriptor_record_stating_another_address_is_refused

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::an_encrypted_record_is_refused_as_encrypted

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::a_record_of_another_version_is_refused

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/bsms.rs::the_record_this_device_writes_parses_back_and_verifies

no fuzz target: Takes a record text; the descriptor it carries is the policy parser, which the policy target fuzzes

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: btclib's bms and btclib-wallet's BIP-32, by the btclib developers, for every key record's signature, made, verified and written; Bitcoin Core v31.1 (deriveaddresses), by the Bitcoin Core developers, for a descriptor record's addresses.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: BIP 129's record lines read in area_import.py, and the rules no outside code has: the paths line (/0/*,/1/* or No path restrictions, read as the two chains, F64), a descriptor that is not a template, a description over eighty characters, a token not BIP 129's, and the header forms a signature is accepted in.

Area import

Faraday's code faraday_intake::wallet::read_wallet (the wallet a file or text is: descriptors, faraday_bip::coldcard, faraday_bip::multisig_config, Caravan's, Electrum's, Cobo's, Keystone's and Wasabi's files, Specter DIY's addwallet, faraday_bip::bsms::DescriptorRecord), and faraday_bip::bsms::SignerRecord read and verified and faraday_bip::bsms::signer_record over the BIP-48 account node's key

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1's bitcoind on regtest, offline (deriveaddresses), and its descriptors.py (BIP-380's checksum) and address.py, for each wallet's receive and change scripts and every checksum; btclib-wallet 2026.10.29's BIP-32 (btclib's own curve) for every key, btclib 2026.10.24's SLIP-132 versions and addresses, and its bms for every key record's signature, made, verified and written byte for byte; the files are written in the area from each format's documentation, and the meaning of their fields is the area's.

Checked first: Core's release tarball against its pinned SHA-256 before every run; btclib's wheels against the hashes btclib-wallet's lock file names (references.sha256), with libsecp256k1 off; BIP 129's own records and the files Sparrow, Nunchuk, Coldcard and Core wrote, judged in the prelude.

The wallet's name a file carries; Electrum's encrypted files and BSMS's encrypted records, which Faraday refuses by name; a silent payments record; the kind of a refusal.

Vectors BIP 129's key and descriptor records (tools/vectors/bip129/), and other wallets' files from their test suites (faraday/faraday-core/tests/vectors/interop/): Sparrow's and Nunchuk's BSMS records, Coldcard Mk4's and Q's exports, a Core listdescriptors, Sparrow's Specter DIY addwallet.

Random cases single-key wallets (P2PKH, P2SH-P2WPKH, P2WPKH, P2TR), sortedmulti in P2WSH, P2SH-P2WSH and P2SH of one to fifteen keys, a miniscript in P2WSH, a taproot tree and a taproot sortedmulti_a, on mainnet and testnet keys, each as a descriptor (multipath with and without checksum, receive and change lines, labelled, receive alone, Core's listdescriptors, Specter Desktop's file, Specter DIY's addwallet with and without its keys' derivations) or as the files that state its kind (Coldcard's export and multisig file, Caravan's, Electrum's, Cobo's, Wasabi's, a key alone, a BSMS descriptor record), in BIP-32's or SLIP-132's spelling; damaged by a wrong checksum, a wrong first address, a SLIP-132 version of another script or another paths line; key records signed by btclib in each header form, with or without damage, and Faraday's own record of random seeds.

Differences Still ours, expected from Faraday's rule: a SLIP-132 version that names another script than the file's (F155), a BSMS paths line other than /0/*,/1/* or No path restrictions, which Faraday reads as the two chains (F64), a BSMS descriptor that is not a template, a key record whose description is over eighty characters or whose token is not BIP 129's; and which account of a Coldcard export, which pair of a listdescriptors, and what each field of every format means, the area's reading of each format's documentation; a checksum in Specter DIY's addwallet, which Specter DIY drops unread and Faraday checks, is refused when Core's code calls it wrong; and the reading of each format's documentation.

A key record's signature in any of BIP-137's header forms is accepted when it recovers the record's key: btclib is asked with the address that form names (Faraday reads no address from the record).

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

SpecificationBIP 173 · BIP 350 · Base58CheckPublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withBitcoin Core's functional-test frameworkOthers' code
Action

Reads an address typed or scanned, and says whether the text so far can still become an address on a network.

Specification

BIP 173, Base32 address format for native v0-16 witness outputs, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: An address of another network is told apart from an invalid one, and a silent payment address is told apart from both. Turn dice into entropy

SpecificationDice hashing of Coldcard and SeedSignerPublishedCodeLibrary: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-entropyCross-checked withColdcard's rolls.py and rolls12.py (public domain)Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Turns dice rolls into entropy by one of three published procedures, and cautions about runs, sequences and skew.

Specification

Dice hashing of Coldcard and SeedSigner, Coldcard rolls.py, SeedSigner, EntropyLab, Keystone and BitBox dice procedures, Convention

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-entropy: Hashed rolls are SHA-256 of the rolls as ASCII digits, truncated to the strength; the second procedure writes every 6 as 0 first; the third names every word from five rolls of 1 to 4 and a sixth read as a coin, and the checksum replaces the last word's low bits.

Checked against

tools/vectors/entropy

Computed by Coldcard's rolls.py and EntropyLab, run as tools/reference/dice/README.md records.

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/keygen.rs::too_few_rolls_make_no_words

no fuzz target: Takes single key presses from a pad, one die face at a time; no text is parsed

Coins: 128, 160, 192, 224 or 256 flips; hex: 32, 40, 48, 56 or 64 digits.

A character that is not a hex digit is refused.

Faraday's own calls

Coins and hex are packed or taken as typed, with no hashing. Neutral.

no decision

Take the device's entropy

SpecificationOwn specificationCodeLibrary: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-entropyCross-checked withFaraday's second version
Action

Hashes the 32 bytes the shell answered the entropy request with into a key's entropy.

Specification

Own specification (no decision)

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-entropy: The entropy is SHA-256 of the 32 bytes, truncated to the strength, so the key is not the shell's bytes themselves; the source trusts the device. Where the person added randomness of their own, the hash is over a label, the 32 bytes and the accumulator of what was added, so someone who lacks either part cannot predict the key.

Keep the session's randomness

SpecificationOwn specificationCodeLibrary: bitcoin_hashes 0.14Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-coreCross-checked withFaraday's second version
Action

Keeps the session's seed and pool from the shell's answers, mixes a person's own randomness in, never in place of the system's, and draws the seed of a MuSig2 round.

Specification

Own specification (F109, F86)

Code

Library: bitcoin_hashes 0.14.101, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-core: The seed is the shell's first answer, hashed with the person's accumulator when there is one and again at each batch added (SHA-256 of a label, the base and the accumulator); the accumulator chains each batch's commitment; a fresh answer is SHA-256 of a label, what it is for, the answer and the accumulator; a signing round's seed is SHA-256 of a label, the session's seed and a count of rounds; nothing is made before the system has answered.

Make a SeedQR

SpecificationSeedQR · BIP 39PublishedCodeLibrary: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Library: unicode-normalization 0.1 (through core/faraday-bip)Faraday's code: core/faraday-codecCross-checked withSeedSigner's encoders and DecodeQROthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Makes a SeedQR or CompactSeedQR picture from a key's words.

Specification

SeedQR, SeedQR and CompactSeedQR, Convention, vendored

BIP 39, Mnemonic code for generating deterministic keys, Deployed

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: unicode-normalization 0.1.25, by the unicode-rs developers, through core/faraday-bip.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-codec: The word indices as four decimal digits each in numeric mode, or the entropy bytes in byte mode, at error-correction level L; the matrix and the digit buffer are wiped; the code is the smallest version that holds the payload. The app writes the picture as an eight-bit greyscale PNG with the key's fingerprint as its title, and wipes the pixels drawn once the file is made; the blank sheet's grid is the SeedQR of an all-zero seed of the same length, so its fixed squares are every seed's.

Checked against

tools/vectors/seedqr

Published, by SeedSigner's SeedQR specification, by the SeedSigner developers.

core/faraday-codec/src/seedqr.rs::specification_vectors_encode_and_decode

core/faraday-codec/src/seedqr.rs::round_trips_in_every_language

core/faraday-codec/src/qr.rs::seedqr_sizes_follow_the_specification

core/faraday-codec/src/qr.rs::a_seedqr_matrix_is_wiped

fuzz seedqr

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: SeedSigner's encoders and DecodeQR, by the SeedSigner developers, over embit's BIP-39 code, by Stepan Snigirev, and Project Nayuki's qrcodegen.py, by Nayuki.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: the CompactSeedQR of 15, 18 and 21 words, which SeedSigner does not define (the entropy itself).

Area seedqr

Faraday's code faraday_codec::seedqr (SeedQR, CompactSeedQR), faraday_codec::qr, the encoder that draws them, and faraday_codec::qr::codex32qr with the picture faraday_core::picture writes of it

Compared with SeedSigner's own encoders and DecodeQR (seedsigner/, unchanged, with embit's BIP-39 code in embit/); Project Nayuki's qrcodegen.py (qrcodegen/), module for module.

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, and a published vector's answer is required of the reference as well as of Faraday; SeedSigner's reader is looser than Faraday (it takes 49 digits, a sign inside a group, 20 to 28 bytes, and does not check the checksum of digits), so Faraday's own rule, the lengths and the digits, stands in front of it in area_seedqr.py and the checksum is embit's; SeedSigner runs in English for every language, the indices being the same; the profile's own pictures are read from their pixels and must be Faraday's code module for module.

Vectors SeedSigner's nine test SeedQRs (tools/vectors/seedqr/); every string and picture of the Codex32QR profile (tools/vectors/codex32qr/).

Random cases Keys of every length and language, both codes and the words read back; digits and bytes malformed; payloads in each mode at each level and version limit; 48-character codex32 strings in either case as Codex32QR codes and the pictures written of them read back, other lengths refused.

Differences SeedQR digits: SeedSigner reads a string with 1 to 3 digits more than 48 or 96 (it drops the tail) and one with a sign or a space inside a group of four (int() takes them); Faraday refuses both (5 cases, each checked on both sides and counted). SeedSigner takes no CompactSeedQR of 20, 24 or 28 bytes either, and strips checksum bits for 12 and 24 words only; the corpus keeps Faraday's 15, 18 and 21 word compact form on the entropy itself.

Codex32QR: Faraday picks the mask Nayuki's encoder picks; the profile's encoder chose another for 6 of its 22 pictures. For those the area swaps the mask of Faraday's code for the picture's with qrcodegen.py's masking, requires the picture, and counts it.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 800 cases

Work with codex32 strings

SpecificationBIP 93PublishedCodeFaraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withpython-codex32Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads, checksums, splits and recovers codex32 strings, and keeps and gathers the strings of a key's set.

Specification

BIP 93, codex32: Checksummed SSS-able BIP32 Seeds, Draft, vendored

Code

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: The BCH checksum and the Lagrange interpolation over GF(32) are written here, with no codex32 crate; the string, its payload and the seed live in fixed arrays wiped on drop. Recovery takes exactly k shares; a further share is derived only from exactly k strings at an index not in use.

Secret handling

Reveal::BackupStick

Not stated by the code: the arithmetic is table lookups over GF(32).

The string, its payload and the seed are fixed arrays wiped on drop; a codex32 share record's string is a secret.

Limits

Seeds of 128, 160, 192, 224, 256 or 512 bits.

A threshold of 2 to 9, or 0 for the secret alone, and at most 31 strings.

A string that does not hold together, or whose checksum fails, is refused with the reason named.

A key of words is never split into codex32 strings.

Faraday's own calls

Recovery is from exactly k shares only. Conservative, F90.

The set's identifier is the first 20 bits of the master fingerprint, which puts 20 bits of it on every share. Neutral.

Correct a codex32 string

SpecificationBIP 93PublishedCodeFaraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withpython-codex32Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Proposes the one correction a codex32 checksum justifies for a damaged string, within BIP 93's bound.

Specification

BIP 93, codex32: Checksummed SSS-able BIP32 Seeds, Draft, vendored

Code

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: BIP 93 specifies no procedure; the decoder is written here after python-codex32's: up to 4 substituted boxes, up to 8 unreadable boxes, any mix with twice the substitutions plus the unreadable boxes at most 8, or one run of 9 to 13 unreadable boxes (15 on the long checksum); the proposal is parsed again before anything takes it.

Checked against

tools/vectors/codex32-correction

Published, by python-codex32's frozen correction corpus, by Ben Westgate.

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/codex32_correct.rs::the_frozen_corpus_is_corrected_or_refused_as_published

core/faraday-bip/tests/all/codex32_correct.rs::ms1_and_the_header_the_set_fixes_are_never_changed

fuzz seed_parts

Area codex32-correct

Faraday's code faraday_bip::codex32::correct

Compared with python-codex32's decoder, correction.py at a later commit (codex32/correction/), asked with BIP 93's bound.

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, and a published vector's answer is required of the reference as well as of Faraday; it is asked with the bound BIP 93 states, written in the area from that sentence and nothing else.

Vectors python-codex32's frozen correction corpus, 57 cases (tools/vectors/codex32-correction/).

Random cases Random strings of every length with substitutions and unreadable boxes up to and past the bound, runs of unreadable boxes around the checksum's length, a held string's header fixed, upper case, ms1 damaged.

Differences Codex32 correction: python-codex32 reads a character outside the bech32 alphabet as unreadable, and fills more than 8 unreadable boxes that are not one run when its equations allow; Faraday refuses both (docs/CODEX32.md §8.4). The corpus damages strings with bech32 characters and ? only.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-bip/src/codex32/correct.rs · correct

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the code.

The strings are fixed arrays wiped on drop.

Limits

ms1 and, where a string of the set is in, the threshold and identifier boxes are never changed.

Insertions, deletions and swapped boxes are not searched for.

A character outside the bech32 alphabet other than ? is refused, not read as unreadable.

More than 8 unreadable boxes that are not one run are refused.

Faraday's own calls

Correction is opt-in, off whenever the form opens, proposes only where the correction is unique, and is never applied: the person types the changed boxes again. Conservative, F94.

A character outside the bech32 alphabet is not read as an erasure, and more than 8 erasures not in one run are refused; BIP 93 makes the first optional, and python-codex32 accepts both. Conservative, F155.

Link to this action

Area seedqr

Faraday's code faraday_codec::seedqr (SeedQR, CompactSeedQR), faraday_codec::qr, the encoder that draws them, and faraday_codec::qr::codex32qr with the picture faraday_core::picture writes of it

Compared with SeedSigner's own encoders and DecodeQR (seedsigner/, unchanged, with embit's BIP-39 code in embit/); Project Nayuki's qrcodegen.py (qrcodegen/), module for module.

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, and a published vector's answer is required of the reference as well as of Faraday; SeedSigner's reader is looser than Faraday (it takes 49 digits, a sign inside a group, 20 to 28 bytes, and does not check the checksum of digits), so Faraday's own rule, the lengths and the digits, stands in front of it in area_seedqr.py and the checksum is embit's; SeedSigner runs in English for every language, the indices being the same; the profile's own pictures are read from their pixels and must be Faraday's code module for module.

Vectors SeedSigner's nine test SeedQRs (tools/vectors/seedqr/); every string and picture of the Codex32QR profile (tools/vectors/codex32qr/).

Random cases Keys of every length and language, both codes and the words read back; digits and bytes malformed; payloads in each mode at each level and version limit; 48-character codex32 strings in either case as Codex32QR codes and the pictures written of them read back, other lengths refused.

Differences SeedQR digits: SeedSigner reads a string with 1 to 3 digits more than 48 or 96 (it drops the tail) and one with a sign or a space inside a group of four (int() takes them); Faraday refuses both (5 cases, each checked on both sides and counted). SeedSigner takes no CompactSeedQR of 20, 24 or 28 bytes either, and strips checksum bits for 12 and 24 words only; the corpus keeps Faraday's 15, 18 and 21 word compact form on the entropy itself.

Codex32QR: Faraday picks the mask Nayuki's encoder picks; the profile's encoder chose another for 6 of its 22 pictures. For those the area swaps the mask of Faraday's code for the picture's with qrcodegen.py's masking, requires the picture, and counts it.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Split with SLIP-39

SpecificationSLIP-0039PublishedCodeLibrary: hmac 0.13 (through core/faraday-crypto)Library: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withpython-shamir-mnemonicOthers' code
Action

Splits a master secret into SLIP-39 shares and recovers it from them, with the passphrase's Feistel encryption.

Specification

SLIP-0039, Shamir's Secret-Sharing for Mnemonic Codes, Status not checked, vendored

Code

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: Written here over faraday-crypto's HMAC-SHA256 and PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256: the RS1024 checksum, Shamir over GF(256), the digest share and the Feistel cipher. The recovered master secret is the BIP 32 seed, not BIP 39 entropy. New key's split takes the shares' identifier and the split's randomness from the session's randomness, each 32 bytes from an answer of the system's of its own; the identifier is 15 bits of the first two bytes.

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

Reveal::PaperForm

Not stated by the code.

The master secret is a Secret; the share values and points wipe on drop.

Limits

Secrets of 16 to 32 bytes of even length.

An iteration exponent above 5 is refused before any PBKDF2 round.

A group threshold of 1 over several groups is refused when splitting.

A wrong passphrase opens a different wallet: the passphrase is not a check.

Faraday's own calls

Shares with an iteration exponent above 5 are refused; a split writes exponent 1 with the extendable flag set. Conservative, F90.

A split with a group threshold of 1 over several groups is refused. Conservative, F90.

Secrets above 256 bits are refused. Conservative, F155.

The split's randomness is taken from the session's randomness, an answer for every 32 bytes; SLIP-0039 does not say where a split's randomness comes from. Neutral, F154.

Action

Recognises a seed or a private key in a file, a scan or pasted text, works out the fingerprint of its key, and refuses a secret where one may not go.

Specification

BIP 39, Mnemonic code for generating deterministic keys, Deployed

BIP 93, codex32: Checksummed SSS-able BIP32 Seeds, Draft, vendored

SLIP-0039, Shamir's Secret-Sharing for Mnemonic Codes, Status not checked, vendored

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: unicode-normalization 0.1.25, by the unicode-rs developers, through core/faraday-bip.

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-core: A words file is read in whichever BIP 39 word list it is in, checksum included; SLIP-39 shares and codex32 strings are gathered from their files and the codex32 seed is recovered from them. Pasted text is refused when it is words of any list, checksum or not, a SeedQR's 48 or 96 digits, an extended private key, a WIF key, a codex32 string or a SLIP-39 share; a scanner that loads a seed refuses only a private key.

Read an aezeed

SpecificationLND aezeed · RFC 7914PublishedCodeFaraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withLightning Labs (LND)Others' code
Action

Reads an LND aezeed: 24 words, checksum, scrypt key and AEZ decipher, giving the entropy, version and birthday; a scanner tells one apart by its version and checksum alone.

Specification

LND aezeed, LND cipher seed (aezeed) with AEZ v5, Convention

RFC 7914, The scrypt Password-Based Key Derivation Function, Status not checked

Code

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: AEZ v5 is written here; the words are 24 x 11 bits with a CRC-32C checked before scrypt (N 32768, r 8, p 1) runs; reading only: an aezeed is never made, and never loaded as a key.

Receive silent payments

SpecificationBIP 352 · BIP 392 · BIP 321 · 1 morePublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: secp256k1 0.29Library: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-bipCross-checked withBIP-352's reference.pyOthers' code
Action

Derives a silent payments address, labels and scan key (BIP 352, 392) and finds the payments a transaction makes to them; reads and writes BIP 392's sp() descriptor.

Specification

BIP 352, Silent Payments, Complete, vendored

BIP 392, Silent Payment Scanning Descriptors, Draft, vendored

BIP 321, URI Scheme for Bitcoin Payments, Complete, vendored

BIP 353, DNS Payment Instructions, Complete, vendored

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-bip: The scan and spend keys are taken at BIP 352's paths; a transaction's eligible inputs give the input hash and shared secret, and every output it pays to the wallet is found with the label it was paid to, up to K_max; the scan private key leaves only as BIP 392's spscan1q text, or the sp() descriptor over it with its key origin, for a named reason. An sp() descriptor, or its key alone after its origin, is read as a silent payments record: the scan key is read for its public key, in constant time, and wiped; an origin that is missing or not BIP 352's path on the key's network is refused, as are sp(KEY,KEY), spspend and a key with a derivation; a text that holds a scan key is a wallet file in Files, and one that holds a spend key a secret no scanner files. The app's check of a transaction takes its previous transactions one at a time and gives the previous outputs, in input order, once every one is known.

Checked against

tools/vectors/bip352

Published, by BIP 352's authors (bitcoin/bips).

tools/vectors/bip392

Published, by the authors of BIP 392, BIP 321 and BIP 353 (bitcoin/bips).

Faraday's own, expected from its rule: an sp() with no key origin, which BIP-392 allows, is refused, as is an origin other than BIP-352's path.

Read a PSBT

SpecificationBIP 174PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: miniscript 12.3 (through core/faraday-bip)Library: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1 (decodepsbtOthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads a version 0 PSBT from bytes or base64 and writes it back as it was read.

Specification

BIP 174, Partially Signed Bitcoin Transaction Format, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: miniscript 12.3.7, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-miniscript), through core/faraday-bip.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: Refuses a version other than 0, version 2 fields inside a version 0 file, a 65-byte taproot signature whose sighash byte is 0 (BIP 341) and a key origin of more than 16 steps; a field not changed since it was read is written back exactly as read. The app tells a file is a PSBT by reading it once as bytes, else as base64 text, when it comes in.

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt

Published, by BIP 174's and RFC 6979's authors; the spends and finalized answers Bitcoin Core v31.1 made on regtest. Faraday's own known answers: the wallet policies, the warning PSBTs and the PSBTs this tree signed.

tools/vectors/psbt-bips

Published, by the authors of BIP 174, BIP 370 and BIP 371 (bitcoin/bips).

fuzz psbt_parse

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: Bitcoin Core v31.1 (decodepsbt, and combinepsbt as its writer), by Bitcoin Core's contributors; HWI's psbt.py, by Ava Chow and HWI's contributors, for the version 2 fields BIP-370 refuses in version 0.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: the refusal of a 65-byte taproot signature whose sighash byte is 0 (BIP-341), which neither Core nor HWI makes, written in area_psbt.py.

Area psbt

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::Psbt (version 0, over bitcoin), faraday_psbt::v2::PsbtV2 (version 2, Faraday's own)

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1's bitcoind on regtest for version 0 (decodepsbt, combinepsbt of one PSBT as its writer, the test framework's taproot_tree_helper); HWI's psbt.py (hwi/) for version 2 and the version 2 fields refused in version 0, its version 0 form read by Core; the refusals neither makes written in area_psbt.py.

Checked first: Core's release tarball is checked against its pinned SHA-256 (tools/reference/core/release.pin) before every run, and the node's version is asked; HWI's vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run; a published vector's answer is required of the side that decides.

Whether a PSBT_GLOBAL_VERSION of 0 is written: Core reads a version 0 PSBT alike with it or without it, and Faraday writes it back as read.

Vectors Every PSBT of BIP-174's, 370's and 371's test vectors (tools/vectors/psbt-bips/), with BIP-370's locktimes.

Random cases Random version 0 and 2 PSBTs with every field Faraday reads, taproot trees among them, in random order, read field for field as Core reads them and written back byte for byte as read and as Core writes them, then broken one rule at a time; each version 0 PSBT made version 2 as HWI's convert_to_v2 makes it and back to its own bytes; BIP-370's locktime, transaction and version 0 form under every mix of required locktimes.

Differences Version 0 made version 2: HWI's convert_to_v2 leaves out the PSBT_OUT_SCRIPT of an output whose script is empty, which BIP-370 requires; Faraday writes it, and the rest of the PSBT must be the same.

Bitcoin Core reads a version 0 PSBT with a version 2 field as valid, the field as unknown; Faraday refuses it, as BIP-370 does, and so does HWI, which decides it.

A taproot signature of 65 bytes whose sighash byte is 0, which neither Core nor HWI refuses, and bytes after a version 2 PSBT's last map or a stream that ends inside one, which HWI reads: Faraday refuses each, as BIP-341 and BIP-174 do, and the refusal is checked by a rule written in the area.

PSBT version 2 (faraday_psbt::v2): a BIP-370 key with key data is an unknown key; BIP-174's and BIP-371's fields inside are checked only when the PSBT is bridged to version 0, except a taproot signature whose sighash byte is 0, refused on reading. HWI checks them on reading; the area requires HWI to read the PSBT without those pairs, to Faraday's locktime and transaction. Where HWI reads a value shorter than its length says and makes a version 0 PSBT of it, which Faraday does not, Core must refuse the version 0 PSBT of the PSBT's own pairs.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Read a version 2 PSBT

SpecificationBIP 370PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withHWI's psbt.py (readingOthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads a version 2 PSBT as its key-value maps, checks BIP 370's shape and converts it to version 0 and back.

Specification

BIP 370, PSBT Version 2, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: bitcoin reads version 0 only, so the maps are read here. Every pair is kept as read and written back with what was set; a version 2 PSBT becomes version 0 once every output has a script, and only the signing fields (partial signatures, final scripts, taproot and MuSig2 signatures and nonces) are taken back from it. A version 0 PSBT becomes version 2 and back to its own bytes. The app reads a version 2 file wherever it reads a PSBT, signs its version 0 form and writes the version 2 PSBT that came in with the signatures added; where they do not go back into it, nothing is written or shown, never the version 0 form.

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt-bips

Published, by the authors of BIP 174, BIP 370 and BIP 371 (bitcoin/bips).

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: the refusals HWI does not make (bytes after the last map, a stream that ends inside a map, a 65-byte taproot signature whose sighash byte is 0), the choice of the pairs Faraday leaves to the bridge to version 0, and the version 0 PSBT of a PSBT's own pairs that Core judges where HWI repairs a short value, written in area_psbt.py.

Area psbt

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::Psbt (version 0, over bitcoin), faraday_psbt::v2::PsbtV2 (version 2, Faraday's own)

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1's bitcoind on regtest for version 0 (decodepsbt, combinepsbt of one PSBT as its writer, the test framework's taproot_tree_helper); HWI's psbt.py (hwi/) for version 2 and the version 2 fields refused in version 0, its version 0 form read by Core; the refusals neither makes written in area_psbt.py.

Checked first: Core's release tarball is checked against its pinned SHA-256 (tools/reference/core/release.pin) before every run, and the node's version is asked; HWI's vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run; a published vector's answer is required of the side that decides.

Whether a PSBT_GLOBAL_VERSION of 0 is written: Core reads a version 0 PSBT alike with it or without it, and Faraday writes it back as read.

Vectors Every PSBT of BIP-174's, 370's and 371's test vectors (tools/vectors/psbt-bips/), with BIP-370's locktimes.

Random cases Random version 0 and 2 PSBTs with every field Faraday reads, taproot trees among them, in random order, read field for field as Core reads them and written back byte for byte as read and as Core writes them, then broken one rule at a time; each version 0 PSBT made version 2 as HWI's convert_to_v2 makes it and back to its own bytes; BIP-370's locktime, transaction and version 0 form under every mix of required locktimes.

Differences Version 0 made version 2: HWI's convert_to_v2 leaves out the PSBT_OUT_SCRIPT of an output whose script is empty, which BIP-370 requires; Faraday writes it, and the rest of the PSBT must be the same.

Bitcoin Core reads a version 0 PSBT with a version 2 field as valid, the field as unknown; Faraday refuses it, as BIP-370 does, and so does HWI, which decides it.

A taproot signature of 65 bytes whose sighash byte is 0, which neither Core nor HWI refuses, and bytes after a version 2 PSBT's last map or a stream that ends inside one, which HWI reads: Faraday refuses each, as BIP-341 and BIP-174 do, and the refusal is checked by a rule written in the area.

PSBT version 2 (faraday_psbt::v2): a BIP-370 key with key data is an unknown key; BIP-174's and BIP-371's fields inside are checked only when the PSBT is bridged to version 0, except a taproot signature whose sighash byte is 0, refused on reading. HWI checks them on reading; the area requires HWI to read the PSBT without those pairs, to Faraday's locktime and transaction. Where HWI reads a value shorter than its length says and makes a version 0 PSBT of it, which Faraday does not, Core must refuse the version 0 PSBT of the PSBT's own pairs.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Review what a PSBT pays

SpecificationBIP 174 · BIP 173 · BIP 350PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: miniscript 12.3Library: sha2 0.11 (through core/faraday-crypto)Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1 (decodepsbtOthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads what a PSBT spends and pays: amounts, fee, verified change, warnings, and the wallet it defines for registering.

Specification

BIP 174, Partially Signed Bitcoin Transaction Format, Deployed

BIP 173, Base32 address format for native v0-16 witness outputs, Deployed

BIP 350, Bech32m Format for v1+ Witness Addresses, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: miniscript 12.3.7, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-miniscript).

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: Change is verified only when the output's script is the address a loaded key's account, or a registered wallet, derives at the stated path; a claim on a loaded key that derives another script is a block. Warnings are ranked Info, Caution, Danger and Blocked. A wallet is offered for registering only when the PSBT's global xpubs give every script of its inputs and outputs. A PSBT's network is read from its global xpubs, else from the coin type of a BIP 44, 49, 84, 86 or 48 key path, else it is the session's. The signed-amount memory keeps the SHA-256 of the unsigned transaction and the amount each input states, and only when every input states one. Another device's signatures are merged only into a PSBT of the same transaction that asks the same sighashes, and are refused when one is invalid. The register sheet's hold registers a wallet only once its descriptor gives the spend's scripts and one of its keys is a loaded key's own at its path. An output over 21,000,000 BTC, outputs whose total is over it, or an amount an input states or their total over it, is a block, as Bitcoin Core's CheckTransaction and CheckTxInputs refuse them (`transaction::money_total`).

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt

Published, by BIP 174's and RFC 6979's authors; the spends and finalized answers Bitcoin Core v31.1 made on regtest. Faraday's own known answers: the wallet policies, the warning PSBTs and the PSBTs this tree signed.

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: Bitcoin Core v31.1 (decodepsbt, analyzepsbt, deriveaddresses), by the Bitcoin Core developers.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: the rule that an output is change when it claims a held key's origins and Core's script at the path it states is the output's own, the rule that a script of several keys, a miniscript or a taproot tree is change only for a registered wallet, and the account keys a registered wallet names, derived in the area.

Area psbt-review

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::inspect (amounts in and out, the fee, verified change and the spoofed-change and missing-UTXO warnings), over faraday_psbt::Psbt and the keys and registered wallets the Sign screen holds

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1's bitcoind on regtest, offline: decodepsbt and analyzepsbt for amounts and fee, deriveaddresses from tprv descriptors for which outputs are change, createpsbt, createrawtransaction and descriptorprocesspsbt to make the PSBTs; the area adds each input's UTXO fields, splices a spoofed output, derives the account keys a registered wallet names (checked by Core deriving the same addresses from them), and applies the rule that an output is change when it claims a held key's origins and Core's script at the path it states is the output's own.

Checked first: Core's release tarball is checked against its pinned SHA-256 (tools/reference/core/release.pin) before every run, and the node's version is asked; every PSBT is one Core made and read, and every change decision is one of Core's derived scripts.

Faraday's other warnings (high fee, dust, address reuse, sighash, network, locktime) and thresholds, which are Faraday's own rules; the estimated virtual size and fee rate (Core's estimate needs signing data the PSBT does not carry).

Vectors none: Core's own decoding and derivation are the reference, over each wallet shape with change, spoofed change, a foreign claim and missing UTXO data in the prelude.

Random cases PSBTs of one to three inputs and one to four outputs over single-key accounts (P2PKH, P2SH-P2WPKH, P2WPKH, P2TR) and registered wallets (sortedmulti in P2WSH, a miniscript in P2WSH, a taproot tree, a taproot sortedmulti_a) or the same wallets unregistered: witness UTXOs, previous transactions and no UTXO data, fees from nothing to a third of the inputs, payments, change on either chain, another wallet's change, and spoofed change over the wallet's own script at another index, another wallet's and a stranger's; one key held, two, or none of the wallet's.

Differences Change of a script of several keys, a miniscript or a taproot tree: Faraday verifies it only for a registered wallet that holds a loaded key, and counts it as paid to others otherwise, which Core has no notion of. The area expects the same refusal, and does not compare the spoof warning for an unregistered wallet (Faraday's reading of such a claim depends on the account path it states).

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/inspect.rs · inspect

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Only public account keys are derived from a master key; the review compares public data. The library does not state the derivation's timing.

The review keeps public keys only; the derived private keys are dropped inside the master key's account_xpub.

Limits

Supported input scripts: p2pkh, p2sh-p2wpkh, p2wpkh, p2tr key path, and multi or sortedmulti in p2sh, p2wsh and p2sh-p2wsh, with miniscript and taproot trees through a registered wallet.

Multisig change is verified only by re-deriving it from a registered wallet.

A SegWit v0 input with no previous transaction in a transaction of several inputs is blocked.

Only a high fee and an absurd fee rate can be held past; every other danger and every block has no way past.

Another device's PSBT for another transaction, one that asks another sighash of an input and one with a signature that does not verify are refused, never merged.

An amount or a total over 21,000,000 BTC, in the outputs or the amounts the inputs state, blocks signing.

Faraday's own calls

Change under an unloaded key, or at a path under no loaded account, counts as a payment to others. Conservative, F136.

A script of several keys is signed only for a registered wallet that holds a loaded key. Conservative, F135.

A miniscript or a taproot tree of one key is signed only through its registered wallet. Conservative, F141.

A SegWit v0 input with only a witness UTXO in a transaction of several inputs is refused, which BIP 174 allows. Conservative, F155.

The signed-amount memory refuses one transaction stated again with other amounts. Conservative, F155.

Review the sighash types

SpecificationBIP 143 · BIP 341 · BIP 342PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core's functional-test frameworkOthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads each input's sighash type and signs SIGHASH_ALL and SIGHASH_DEFAULT, and ALL with ANYONECANPAY after a caution.

Specification

BIP 143, Transaction Signature Verification for Version 0 Witness Program, Deployed

BIP 341, Taproot: SegWit version 1 spending rules, Deployed

BIP 342, Validation of Taproot Scripts, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: Names the sighash kind of every input; ALL|ANYONECANPAY is a caution, NONE, a non-standard value and SINGLE with an output are dangers with no way past, SINGLE with no output of its index is blocked. The tx area checks the signature hashes bitcoin computes for each type; the naming and ranking of a sighash is not cross-checked.

A raw transaction whose witness stack counts more items than the transaction has bytes is refused in the area: Core's framework reads past the end as zeros and would take hours to write such a stack back. Faraday's full reading refuses it too, since every item is written back as at least one byte, and the harness's own tests check the shortcut.

Sign with ECDSA

SpecificationBIP 143 · BIP 174 · RFC 6979PublishedCodeLibrary: secp256k1 0.29Library: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Signs a transaction input with ECDSA, from the key the PSBT's origin names, and checks the key against the script.

Specification

BIP 143, Transaction Signature Verification for Version 0 Witness Program, Deployed

BIP 174, Partially Signed Bitcoin Transaction Format, Deployed

RFC 6979, Deterministic Usage of the Digital Signature Algorithm (DSA) and Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (ECDSA), Informational

Code

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: Derives the key at the origin's path from the matching master key and refuses unless it is the key named and the script holds it; grinds for a low r by default; signs twice and refuses unless the bytes match; verifies the signature before writing it. faraday-keys lends the master keys and passes the default nonce and aux_rand choices.

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt

Published, by BIP 174's and RFC 6979's authors; the spends and finalized answers Bitcoin Core v31.1 made on regtest. Faraday's own known answers: the wallet policies, the warning PSBTs and the PSBTs this tree signed.

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/nonce.rs::rfc6979_cross_implementation_vectors
