core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/nonce.rs::low_r_gives_the_shortest_signature

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/sign.rs::sign_verify_finalize_every_script_type

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/sign.rs::key_origin_that_does_not_match_the_derived_key_is_refused

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/sign.rs::signing_needs_a_matching_master_key

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/multisig.rs::a_cosigner_key_under_a_loaded_fingerprint_is_not_ours

self-test ecdsa_vector

no fuzz target: No target runs sign: it needs a master key and a PSBT that inspect accepts; psbt_parse fuzzes the PSBT it reads

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: Bitcoin Core v31.1, by the Bitcoin Core developers (descriptorprocesspsbt signs, testmempoolaccept judges the spend), with Core's key.py and script.py for the first RFC 6979 nonce; python-ecdsa, by Brian Warner and Hubert Kario (no Bitcoin code), which makes that nonce's signature again byte for byte and verifies the low-R ones.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: Faraday's refusals (F136, F141) and the inputs a wallet's rule leaves unsigned, the script code of the hash the first RFC 6979 nonce is checked under, in area_sign.py; the keys of those checks are btclib's.

Area sign

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::sign (every held key selected, deterministic Schnorr, both RFC 6979 nonce rules) and faraday_psbt::finalize

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1 on regtest (core_node.py): descriptorprocesspsbt signs from the held masters' keys, finalizepsbt finalizes and extracts, testmempoolaccept judges each finalized transaction over its funding on the node; BIP-340's reference (secp256k1lab) and Core's key.py for the deterministic nonces, over Core's script.py sighashes; python-ecdsa (the distribution's package) and btclib (btclib/, its own curve) as a second curve lineage, beside them: every ECDSA signature again under plain RFC 6979 or verified, every BIP-340 signature verified and made again; python-bip380 (miniscript/) reads the leaves. Faraday's refusals and the inputs a wallet's rule leaves unsigned are ours, in area_sign.py.

Checked first: Core is the pinned release, hashed against its pin before every run; the vendored files are hashed against references.sha256; a published vector's answer is required of Core as well as of Faraday; every ECDSA and BIP-340 signature Core makes is checked to verify under the hash the area computes before that hash is used for Faraday's deterministic nonces.

Vectors BIP-174's signer, finalizer and extractor vectors; the Core-built spends of tools/vectors/psbt/ (2-of-3 sortedmulti, the Liana wallet down both paths, a taproot tree by a leaf and by the key path, BIP 387's taproot multisig), signed by every subset of their keys; the finalizer vectors there (or_b's smaller signature, the taproot leaves that tie by count, the taproot multisig and MuSig2 spends).

Random cases PSBTs of one to four inputs, each spending a funding output on the node: P2PKH, P2SH-P2WPKH, P2WPKH, multi/sortedmulti in P2SH, P2WSH and P2SH-P2WSH, P2TR key path, P2TR script path (pk, pkh, timelocked keys, or_d, multi_a) with the key path beside it, miniscript in P2WSH and P2SH-P2WSH; held and foreign keys, cosigners' signatures already on (Core's), every standard sighash type, timelocks met and not; Core's signatures (ECDSA byte for byte, BIP-340 by verification and the deterministic nonce), each final scriptSig and witness and the extracted transaction Core's, the cleared fields, and every finalized transaction accepted by Core's mempool; an origin that derives another key refused; sweeps: a WIF key's P2PKH, P2WPKH and P2SH-P2WPKH outputs (P2PKH alone for an uncompressed key) spent beside another key's, signed by Core from the WIF and by faraday_psbt::sweep::sign, byte for byte, finalized and accepted by the mempool.

Differences PSBT signing: Faraday refuses to sign a SegWit v0 input with no previous transaction in a transaction of more than one input; Core signs it, and BIP-174's own signer vector is such a PSBT. The refusal is ours, checked and counted. Faraday holds a finalized input to the same rule and refuses the PSBT when such an input is final, except a taproot one, whose final witness is the coordinator's.

PSBT signing, wallets: Faraday signs an input spent from a script of several keys (a multi of two or more, a miniscript naming two or more, a taproot script path whose carried leaves name two or more with its internal key, or are not its whole tree) only for a registered wallet that holds one of its keys and whose script it is; Core signs any. The area refuses, by a rule of its own, where no wallet the request registers holds a held key and gives the input, and counts it only where Faraday's refusal is that the wallet is not registered. A miniscript or a taproot tree of one key is signed only through such a wallet, and is otherwise left unsigned while the rest is signed (F141); the area takes Core's signatures off it. Half the corpus's multi, miniscript and taproot-tree inputs come with their wallet registered, so their signatures are still compared. A registered tree's every leaf has its wait met, since Faraday refuses a path that can never be valid, which the area does not work out.

PSBT signing: Faraday refuses a PSBT any input of which asks for a NONE or SINGLE sighash, and one that none of the held keys can sign: dangers no hold accepts (F136). Core signs the first and answers the second with nothing signed. The area expects both refusals by a rule of its own, counting a held key as taking part as Faraday's inspection does (a miniscript or a taproot tree only through a wallet in use), checks that Faraday's refusal names the danger, and counts them.

Taproot pkh leaves: Faraday signs a taproot leaf that checks its key by hash but does not finalize it: miniscript 12's finalizer looks the key up among the ECDSA origins and panics, and would push 33 bytes where the leaf hashed 32. Core finalizes such a leaf; the area takes the signatures under it off before Core finalizes, and counts each input Core would otherwise have finalized another way.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 158 cases

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sign.rs · sign

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sign.rs · sign_ecdsa

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

libsecp256k1 documents its signing as constant time; the low-R grind repeats the signing until r is small, so the number of signings varies, which the library does not address.

The derived key lives in the signing function's frame and is wiped when it is dropped; no secret leaves faraday-keys.

Limits

ALL is signed, and ALL with ANYONECANPAY after a caution; NONE, SINGLE and non-standard sighashes are refused.

A SegWit v0 input with no previous transaction, in a transaction of several inputs, is refused.

A key comes from the PSBT's origin only; a multisig key with no origin is refused.

The app signs with the low-R nonce; the first RFC 6979 nonce is the library's other choice and is not offered in the app.

Faraday's own calls

A key comes from the PSBT's origin only, under the master key's fingerprint; a key whose derived public key differs from the one named, or that the script does not hold, is refused, and an origin under an account's fingerprint matches no key. Conservative, F155.

A script of several keys is signed only for a registered wallet that holds a loaded key. Conservative, F135.

Only a high fee and an absurd fee rate can be held past; every other danger refuses. Conservative, F136.

Each signature is made twice and compared, and verified before it is written. Neutral.

no decision

Low-R grinding is the default nonce. Neutral.

no decision

Sign a WIF key's sweep

SpecificationBIP 143 · BIP 174 · RFC 6979PublishedCodeLibrary: secp256k1 0.29Library: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1Others' code
Action

Signs the inputs of a PSBT that spend a WIF key's own outputs, after a review that names each output's loaded wallet.

Specification

BIP 143, Transaction Signature Verification for Version 0 Witness Program, Deployed

BIP 174, Partially Signed Bitcoin Transaction Format, Deployed

RFC 6979, Deterministic Usage of the Digital Signature Algorithm (DSA) and Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (ECDSA), Informational

Code

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: An input is the key's when the output it spends pays one of the key's scripts, not by a key origin; the PSBT is inspected and a block, or a danger that is not holdable, refuses it, but for no loaded master key taking part; every input of the key is checked before the first is signed; each signature is made twice with the low-R nonce, compared and verified before it is written. The review searches each loaded wallet's first hundred addresses on each chain for every output and flags the others.

Checked against

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1 on regtest (core_node.py): descriptorprocesspsbt signs from the held masters' keys, finalizepsbt finalizes and extracts, testmempoolaccept judges each finalized transaction over its funding on the node; BIP-340's reference (secp256k1lab) and Core's key.py for the deterministic nonces, over Core's script.py sighashes; python-ecdsa (the distribution's package) and btclib (btclib/, its own curve) as a second curve lineage, beside them: every ECDSA signature again under plain RFC 6979 or verified, every BIP-340 signature verified and made again; python-bip380 (miniscript/) reads the leaves. Faraday's refusals and the inputs a wallet's rule leaves unsigned are ours, in area_sign.py.

Checked first: Core is the pinned release, hashed against its pin before every run; the vendored files are hashed against references.sha256; a published vector's answer is required of Core as well as of Faraday; every ECDSA and BIP-340 signature Core makes is checked to verify under the hash the area computes before that hash is used for Faraday's deterministic nonces.

Vectors BIP-174's signer, finalizer and extractor vectors; the Core-built spends of tools/vectors/psbt/ (2-of-3 sortedmulti, the Liana wallet down both paths, a taproot tree by a leaf and by the key path, BIP 387's taproot multisig), signed by every subset of their keys; the finalizer vectors there (or_b's smaller signature, the taproot leaves that tie by count, the taproot multisig and MuSig2 spends).

Random cases PSBTs of one to four inputs, each spending a funding output on the node: P2PKH, P2SH-P2WPKH, P2WPKH, multi/sortedmulti in P2SH, P2WSH and P2SH-P2WSH, P2TR key path, P2TR script path (pk, pkh, timelocked keys, or_d, multi_a) with the key path beside it, miniscript in P2WSH and P2SH-P2WSH; held and foreign keys, cosigners' signatures already on (Core's), every standard sighash type, timelocks met and not; Core's signatures (ECDSA byte for byte, BIP-340 by verification and the deterministic nonce), each final scriptSig and witness and the extracted transaction Core's, the cleared fields, and every finalized transaction accepted by Core's mempool; an origin that derives another key refused; sweeps: a WIF key's P2PKH, P2WPKH and P2SH-P2WPKH outputs (P2PKH alone for an uncompressed key) spent beside another key's, signed by Core from the WIF and by faraday_psbt::sweep::sign, byte for byte, finalized and accepted by the mempool.

Differences PSBT signing: Faraday refuses to sign a SegWit v0 input with no previous transaction in a transaction of more than one input; Core signs it, and BIP-174's own signer vector is such a PSBT. The refusal is ours, checked and counted. Faraday holds a finalized input to the same rule and refuses the PSBT when such an input is final, except a taproot one, whose final witness is the coordinator's.

PSBT signing, wallets: Faraday signs an input spent from a script of several keys (a multi of two or more, a miniscript naming two or more, a taproot script path whose carried leaves name two or more with its internal key, or are not its whole tree) only for a registered wallet that holds one of its keys and whose script it is; Core signs any. The area refuses, by a rule of its own, where no wallet the request registers holds a held key and gives the input, and counts it only where Faraday's refusal is that the wallet is not registered. A miniscript or a taproot tree of one key is signed only through such a wallet, and is otherwise left unsigned while the rest is signed (F141); the area takes Core's signatures off it. Half the corpus's multi, miniscript and taproot-tree inputs come with their wallet registered, so their signatures are still compared. A registered tree's every leaf has its wait met, since Faraday refuses a path that can never be valid, which the area does not work out.

PSBT signing: Faraday refuses a PSBT any input of which asks for a NONE or SINGLE sighash, and one that none of the held keys can sign: dangers no hold accepts (F136). Core signs the first and answers the second with nothing signed. The area expects both refusals by a rule of its own, counting a held key as taking part as Faraday's inspection does (a miniscript or a taproot tree only through a wallet in use), checks that Faraday's refusal names the danger, and counts them.

Taproot pkh leaves: Faraday signs a taproot leaf that checks its key by hash but does not finalize it: miniscript 12's finalizer looks the key up among the ECDSA origins and panics, and would push 33 bytes where the leaf hashed 32. Core finalizes such a leaf; the area takes the signatures under it off before Core finalizes, and counts each input Core would otherwise have finalized another way.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sweep.rs · sign_input

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sweep.rs · inputs_of

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

libsecp256k1 documents its signing as constant time; the low-R grind repeats the signing until r is small, so the number of signings varies, which the library does not address.

The key never leaves faraday-keys; it is erased when the sweep ends.

Limits

P2PKH, P2WPKH and P2SH-P2WPKH outputs of the key are signed; a legacy input needs its previous transaction.

An output to no loaded wallet's address is flagged, and the hold signs past it.

Faraday's own calls

An output to no loaded wallet's first hundred addresses on either chain is flagged on the review, and the hold signs past it. Neutral, F171.

Each signature is made twice and compared, and verified before it is written. Neutral.

no decision

Sign with Schnorr

SpecificationBIP 340 · BIP 341 · BIP 342PublishedCodeLibrary: secp256k1 0.29Library: bitcoin 0.32Library: hmac 0.13 (through core/faraday-crypto)1 moreFaraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Signs a taproot input with BIP 340, by the key path or under a tap leaf's hash.

Specification

BIP 340, Schnorr Signatures for secp256k1, Deployed

BIP 341, Taproot: SegWit version 1 spending rules, Deployed

BIP 342, Validation of Taproot Scripts, Deployed

Code

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: Signs the key path with the BIP 341 tweaked key and a leaf with the leaf's own key under its leaf hash; each signature is made twice, compared and verified; aux_rand is zero by default, and fresh bytes a caller passes are expanded for each input with HKDF-Expand (RFC 5869, faraday-crypto), which the app does not use; a miniscript or a tree is signed only through a registered wallet.

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt

Published, by BIP 174's and RFC 6979's authors; the spends and finalized answers Bitcoin Core v31.1 made on regtest. Faraday's own known answers: the wallet policies, the warning PSBTs and the PSBTs this tree signed.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sign.rs · sign_taproot

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sign.rs · sign_tap_leaf

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

libsecp256k1 documents its signing as constant time.

bitcoin forces two copies of the key for Schnorr, the untweaked and the tweaked keypair; both are overwritten with secp256k1's non_secure_erase before the function returns, which the library calls best effort.

Limits

SIGHASH_DEFAULT and ALL are signed, and ALL with ANYONECANPAY after a caution; NONE, SINGLE and undefined types are refused.

A taproot input that no key of this device can sign is left unsigned.

A leaf is signed only when the leaf's script checks a signature against the key, read through the miniscript.

Faraday's own calls

aux_rand is zero, so a signature can be made again byte for byte; BIP 340 recommends fresh randomness against fault attacks. The library offers fresh aux_rand, and the app does not use it. Neutral, F156.

A miniscript or a taproot tree of one key is signed only through its registered wallet. Conservative, F141.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/sign.rs · sign_musig

0.1.9 · core/faraday-bip/src/musig.rs · nonce_gen

0.1.9 · core/faraday-bip/src/musig.rs · sign

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

The secret scalar multiplication is documented in faraday-bip as taking the same time for every scalar and blinded by the context; the code documents no other timing.

A SecNonce is wiped on drop and consumed by the signature it makes; the session wipes its nonces when another transaction opens a session, and holds none once each has signed.

Limits

Key path only; a nonce or partial signature keyed by a tapleaf hash is a script-path spend and is refused.

The wallet must be registered; a participant set no registered wallet has is blocked.

Rounds across a lock, with the secret nonce kept in a vault, are not offered (F109).

Faraday's own calls

Each nonce's rand' is a tagged hash of the session's seed, the input's index and the participant's key: a fresh value per call from the session's seed chain. Neutral.

no decision

The secret nonce is held in memory for its one session and never serialised. Conservative, F109.

Check a PSBT's signatures

SpecificationRFC 6979 · BIP 143 · BIP 341PublishedCodeLibrary: secp256k1 0.29Library: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withNot cross-checked
Action

Checks every signature a PSBT or a finished transaction carries, finds a nonce used twice, recomputes this device's signatures and compares two PSBTs.

Specification

RFC 6979, Deterministic Usage of the Digital Signature Algorithm (DSA) and Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (ECDSA), Informational

BIP 143, Transaction Signature Verification for Version 0 Witness Program, Deployed

BIP 341, Taproot: SegWit version 1 spending rules, Deployed

Code

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: Each signature is checked against the key it is under and the sighash the input's fields give, including the ones inside a finalized witness; one r or one R under one key twice is reported; a signature under a key this device holds is recomputed to say which nonce rule produced it.

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt

Published, by BIP 174's and RFC 6979's authors; the spends and finalized answers Bitcoin Core v31.1 made on regtest. Faraday's own known answers: the wallet policies, the warning PSBTs and the PSBTs this tree signed.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/verify.rs · verify_signatures

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/verify.rs · deterministic

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Private keys are used only to recompute this device's signatures; what comes back is a verdict. The library does not state the recomputation's timing.

The keypairs made to recompute a signature are overwritten with secp256k1's non_secure_erase, which the library calls best effort.

Limits

A signature with no previous output to check against is not checked.

A signature under a sighash type that was not asked for is flagged.

A signature of another device is not recomputed.

Faraday's own calls

The nonce check is run on signatures a device writes, comparing them with the RFC 6979 or BIP 340 nonce rule. Neutral.

no decision

Finalize a transaction

SpecificationBIP 174PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Library: miniscript 12.3Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core v31.1Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Builds the final scriptSig and witness from the partial signatures and extracts the transaction.

Specification

BIP 174, Partially Signed Bitcoin Transaction Format, Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: miniscript 12.3.7, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-miniscript).

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: bitcoin has no finalizer, so single-key and plain multisig inputs are finalized here; a miniscript or a tree goes through miniscript's finalizer with its interpreter check; among satisfactions of a wsh miniscript the smaller by real signature size is chosen, as Bitcoin Core's finalizepsbt chooses it, a multi() keeping its k smallest signatures; the signing fields are cleared as BIP 174 requires. The app keeps the finished transaction and writes it as hex.

Checked against

tools/vectors/psbt

Published, by BIP 174's and RFC 6979's authors; the spends and finalized answers Bitcoin Core v31.1 made on regtest. Faraday's own known answers: the wallet policies, the warning PSBTs and the PSBTs this tree signed.

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/satisfaction.rs::taproot_leaves_that_weigh_the_same_keep_the_first_script_by_bytes

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/miniscript.rs::the_liana_spend_completes_with_the_first_key_alone

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/tapmulti.rs::two_of_the_three_keys_sign_and_the_input_finalizes

Differences PSBT signing: Faraday refuses to sign a SegWit v0 input with no previous transaction in a transaction of more than one input; Core signs it, and BIP-174's own signer vector is such a PSBT. The refusal is ours, checked and counted. Faraday holds a finalized input to the same rule and refuses the PSBT when such an input is final, except a taproot one, whose final witness is the coordinator's.

PSBT signing, wallets: Faraday signs an input spent from a script of several keys (a multi of two or more, a miniscript naming two or more, a taproot script path whose carried leaves name two or more with its internal key, or are not its whole tree) only for a registered wallet that holds one of its keys and whose script it is; Core signs any. The area refuses, by a rule of its own, where no wallet the request registers holds a held key and gives the input, and counts it only where Faraday's refusal is that the wallet is not registered. A miniscript or a taproot tree of one key is signed only through such a wallet, and is otherwise left unsigned while the rest is signed (F141); the area takes Core's signatures off it. Half the corpus's multi, miniscript and taproot-tree inputs come with their wallet registered, so their signatures are still compared. A registered tree's every leaf has its wait met, since Faraday refuses a path that can never be valid, which the area does not work out.

PSBT signing: Faraday refuses a PSBT any input of which asks for a NONE or SINGLE sighash, and one that none of the held keys can sign: dangers no hold accepts (F136). Core signs the first and answers the second with nothing signed. The area expects both refusals by a rule of its own, counting a held key as taking part as Faraday's inspection does (a miniscript or a taproot tree only through a wallet in use), checks that Faraday's refusal names the danger, and counts them.

Taproot pkh leaves: Faraday signs a taproot leaf that checks its key by hash but does not finalize it: miniscript 12's finalizer looks the key up among the ECDSA origins and panics, and would push 33 bytes where the leaf hashed 32. Core finalizes such a leaf; the area takes the signatures under it off before Core finalizes, and counts each input Core would otherwise have finalized another way.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 158 cases

Source

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/finalize.rs · finalize

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

No secret is handled.

Nothing secret is held.

Limits

An input that has too few signatures stays partial and the transaction is not extracted.

A timelock the transaction does not meet stops the input in miniscript's interpreter check.

A final input whose funding transaction lacks the spent output is refused.

A taproot leaf holding pkh() is signed but left for the coordinator to finalize.

Faraday's own calls

Of several satisfactions of a wsh miniscript, the one with the smaller real signature size is chosen; where two weigh the same, the one Bitcoin Core keeps: an or's first branch, a multi()'s keys named first, a thresh()'s branches named last, a taproot spend's leaf whose script sorts first by its bytes. Neutral, F161.

Action

Signs a message with the key at a path, in BIP 137's compact form or as BIP 322 simple.

Specification

BIP 137, Signatures of Messages using Private Keys, Deployed

BIP 322, Generic Signed Message Format, Complete

RFC 6979, Deterministic Usage of the Digital Signature Algorithm (DSA) and Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (ECDSA), Informational

BIP 340, Schnorr Signatures for secp256k1, Deployed

Code

Library: secp256k1 0.29.1, by the rust-bitcoin developers (rust-secp256k1), over libsecp256k1, by Bitcoin Core's libsecp256k1 project (bitcoin-core/secp256k1).

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers, through core/faraday-crypto.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: The address is derived from the key that signs, so the address in the result is the one the signature verifies against; BIP 137 low-R is ground here, libsecp256k1 having no low-R recoverable signer; the key's own blinded curve context is used. In the app the key is the one behind an address of a loaded single-key wallet's receive chain, and a wallet of several keys is refused.

Published, by BIP 322's authors (bitcoin/bips); btcd's BIP-322 (pull request 2521); Trezor's and Electrum's test suites, for BIP 137.

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: Bitcoin Core v31.1 (signmessagewithprivkey, verifymessage, signrawtransactionwithkey); btclib's bms and btclib-wallet's bip322, by the btclib developers, on btclib's own curve; btcd's bip322 (pull request 2521), by Oliver Gugger and the btcd developers; python-ecdsa, by Brian Warner and Hubert Kario, for the first-nonce ECDSA signature of BIP-137.

Area msg

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::message (BIP-137, BIP-322 simple: sign, verify, virtual txids)

Compared with Bitcoin Core v31.1 and btclib for BIP-137, btclib-wallet and btcd for BIP-322: Core's signmessagewithprivkey, verifymessage and signrawtransactionwithkey (the low-R P2WPKH witness); btclib's bms and btclib-wallet's bip322 (btclib/, btclib's own curve); python-ecdsa (the distribution's package) for the first-nonce ECDSA signature of BIP-137; btcd's bip322 from pull request 2521 (btcd-bip322/, Go).

Checked first: The wheels and the Go tree are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, Core's release against its pin; BIP-322's basic and generated vectors and btcd's negative vectors are required of both BIP-322 references, each in the state the vector names, and Trezor's and Electrum's BIP-137 signatures of btclib.

A taproot signature's bytes (BIP-340's auxiliary randomness is the signer's: Faraday's is verified by both BIP-322 references, and btcd's by Faraday); the kind of a refusal (Malformed, Invalid, Unsupported…), Faraday's own words, checked by its tests; Core's reading of a BIP-137 header outside 27 to 42.

Vectors BIP-322's basic and generated vectors (hashes, txids, simple, full and proof-of-funds signatures, errors) and btcd's negative vectors; Trezor's and Electrum's BIP-137 signatures (tools/vectors/message/), Trezor's made again from its keys.

Random cases Random keys, address forms, networks and messages: virtual txids; signatures made byte for byte under both nonce rules and checked by every reference, then checked against a changed message, another address, a changed byte and a moved header; btcd's own simple signatures checked by Faraday.

Differences BIP-137: Faraday reads the address form from the address rather than the header's range and tries a P2PKH address in both key encodings (faraday_psbt::message), as Electrum and Sparrow do; btclib holds the ranges (with Electrum's for SegWit) and Core reads the key encoding from the header. Counted where btclib accepts the signature with the header the address's form names, or Core with the other key encoding.

BIP-322: Faraday refuses the full form and proof of funds, which both references make and accept (for P2PKH and P2SH-P2WPKH addresses they sign in the full form); counted where both accept and Faraday refuses the format.

BIP-322: Faraday checks simple signatures for P2WPKH and P2TR addresses only; a P2WSH script both references accept is refused as an unsupported address, and counted.

Between the references: btclib's interpreter holds witness v0 scripts alone to MINIMALIF, so it accepts btcd's two negative vectors of a legacy P2SH script, in the full form, whose OP_IF takes a non-minimal true; btcd must call them invalid and Faraday, which reads no full form, refuse them, and each is counted.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/message.rs · sign

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

libsecp256k1 documents its signing as constant time; the BIP 137 low-R grind repeats the signing until r is small.

The secret key is borrowed for the moment it signs; the two Schnorr copies bitcoin forces for a taproot address are overwritten with secp256k1's non_secure_erase before the function returns, which the library calls best effort.

Limits

BIP 322 simple only: no full form, no proof of funds.

A nested SegWit address has no BIP 322 simple form and is signed as BIP 137 only.

A taproot address is signed as BIP 322 only.

Faraday's own calls

The app signs with the low-R nonce and zero aux_rand for a taproot address. Neutral, F156.

Read a transaction

SpecificationBIP 141 · BIP 144PublishedCodeLibrary: bitcoin 0.32Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbtCross-checked withBitcoin Core's functional-test frameworkOthers' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Reads a raw transaction, or a PSBT's transaction, to its txid, wtxid, weight and the previous outputs its source states.

Specification

BIP 141, Segregated Witness (Consensus layer), Deployed

BIP 144, Segregated Witness (Peer Services), Deployed

Code

Library: bitcoin 0.32.102, by the rust-bitcoin developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-psbt: A raw transaction is read from hex or bytes by rust-bitcoin, and one it reads to no inputs is refused, as Bitcoin Core refuses those bytes (the segwit flag with no witness); a previous transaction is accepted for an input only when its txid is the one spent. The Decode screen takes the amounts its inputs spend from the spend's PSBT and the Inbox's PSBTs whose unsigned transaction is exactly the one decoded, and uses an input's amount only when no two of them state different ones. An output is named by its script kind (P2WPKH, P2WSH, P2TR, P2SH, P2PKH or OP_RETURN) and marked when it pays to a loaded wallet's first 100 addresses on either chain. A text is taken as a transaction in hex only when it has an even number of at least 120 hex digits that read as a transaction. Decode refuses a transaction with an output over 21,000,000 BTC or outputs whose total is over it, as Bitcoin Core's CheckTransaction does (bad-txns-vout-toolarge, bad-txns-txouttotal-toolarge), and one whose inputs' stated amounts, or their total, are over it, as its CheckTxInputs does (bad-txns-inputvalues-outofrange), so no total or fee it shows overflows.

Checked against

not checked against vectors

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/transaction.rs::a_raw_transaction_is_wrapped_and_keeps_its_id

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/transaction.rs::a_previous_transaction_with_another_id_does_not_state_the_spent_output

core/faraday-psbt/tests/all/transaction.rs::a_transaction_core_refuses_for_a_superfluous_witness_flag_is_refused

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/finish.rs::decode_refuses_amounts_over_21_million_bitcoin_as_core_does

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/finish.rs::decode_refuses_an_amount_going_in_over_21_million_bitcoin

fuzz transaction

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: Bitcoin Core's functional-test framework, v31.1, by Bitcoin Core's contributors.

Compared, for one part, with Faraday's second version, written by Faraday's authors in Python; that part is not checked against an outside source: the rule that a raw transaction is read only when its bytes are exactly the serialisation of what was read, where the framework reads past a short input, written in area_tx.py.

Area tx

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::transaction::read_raw, bitcoin's SighashCache as faraday_psbt::sign calls it

Compared with Core's messages.py and script.py (core/).

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, and a published vector's answer is required of the reference as well as of Faraday; each published signature hash is checked against the reference first.

Vectors Core's 500 legacy sighashes, BIP-143's thirteen, BIP-341's key-path sighashes (tools/vectors/sighash/).

Random cases Random transactions with and without witnesses (txid, wtxid, weight, bytes), cut or lengthened; every input hashed as legacy, BIP-143 P2WPKH and P2WSH, BIP-341 key and script path under every hash type.

Differences Legacy sighash: bitcoin hashes the script code as given, Core removes every OP_CODESEPARATOR first. Faraday's legacy spends (P2PKH, P2SH multisig) have none.

A raw transaction whose witness stack counts more items than the transaction has bytes is refused in the area: Core's framework reads past the end as zeros and would take hours to write such a stack back. Faraday's full reading refuses it too, since every item is written back as at least one byte, and the harness's own tests check the shortcut.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Differences SeedQR digits: SeedSigner reads a string with 1 to 3 digits more than 48 or 96 (it drops the tail) and one with a sign or a space inside a group of four (int() takes them); Faraday refuses both (5 cases, each checked on both sides and counted). SeedSigner takes no CompactSeedQR of 20, 24 or 28 bytes either, and strips checksum bits for 12 and 24 words only; the corpus keeps Faraday's 15, 18 and 21 word compact form on the entropy itself.

Codex32QR: Faraday picks the mask Nayuki's encoder picks; the profile's encoder chose another for 6 of its 22 pictures. For those the area swaps the mask of Faraday's code for the picture's with qrcodegen.py's masking, requires the picture, and counts it.

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the encoder or by rqrr.

The module grid, the bit stream and the data and error-correction codewords wipe on drop; a decoded payload is a zeroizing buffer. The caller asks faraday-keys for any secret it encodes.

Limits

One segment per code; the caller picks numeric, alphanumeric or byte mode.

The error-correction level is never raised above the one asked for.

A frame whose byte count is not width times height yields nothing; a code whose error correction fails is skipped.

Faraday's own calls

The error-correction level is never boosted, so a SeedQR comes out at the level its specification names. Neutral.

Writes and reads UR codes: bytewords with CRC-32, fountain-coded parts and the CBOR around them.

BCR-2020-005, Uniform Resources (UR), the animated-QR transport, Convention, vendored

Differences UR: Faraday has no minimum fragment length, writes a one-fragment message as a part like any other, and refuses to animate an empty message; foundation-ur-py checks a UR type one character's way ('a' <= type <= 'z'). The area asks the reference with a minimum of 1, makes parts with its fountain encoder, and keeps its types to letters, digits and - not starting with z. Faraday's decoder reduces mixes by each other whichever arrived first, and so sometimes finishes a set URKit's does not; the area then checks the message is the one sent and counts the set.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Vaults

8 actions · 5 own specification · 6 cross-checked with others' code · 4 with Faraday's second version · 2 not cross-checked

ActionSpecificationCodeCross-checked with

How the vault is built

SpecificationOwn specificationCodeSpecification · 3 actions

The file

A vault is one file: a header of 53 bytes, then four slots of L + 40 bytes each, 53 + 4 × (L + 40) bytes in all. L is a slot's plaintext length, chosen when the vault is made and fixed for its life:

LFile
65 536262 357
262 1441 048 789
1 048 5764 194 517
4 194 30416 777 429

Every vault has four slots, whether it has one passphrase or four.

The file's name ends in .fvlt.

The header

The header is the first 53 bytes. Its integers are little-endian. Nothing in it is secret, and none of it is a key.

AtBytesWhat
04magic
41version, 1
54Argon2id memory, in KiB
94Argon2id passes
134Argon2id lanes
174L
2132Argon2id salt

The magic is FVLT.

The keys

The passphrase is stretched by Argon2id version 1.3 (RFC 9106) under the header's salt, memory, passes and lanes, to 32 bytes. HKDF-SHA256 (RFC 5869), with an empty salt and the label "Faraday vault v1 slot", makes the slot key from them. One passphrase gives one slot key, which is tried on every slot.

The passphrase is first put in Unicode Normalization Form C (UAX #15), and Argon2id takes that form's UTF-8 bytes. Then "é" typed as one character (U+00E9) and as "e" with a combining accent (U+0065 U+0301) open the same slot, and two passphrases with one Form C are refused as the same when a vault is made. Nothing else is changed: case, spaces and every character are kept as typed, and Form C keeps full-width letters and ligatures as they are: "Ａ" is not "A".

The slots

Each slot is a 24-byte nonce, L bytes of ciphertext and a 16-byte Poly1305 tag, sealed with XChaCha20-Poly1305 (draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha-03). Its associated data is the whole header and the slot's number, zero to three: 54 bytes. A slot's plaintext is a format byte, the slot's records, an end record, and zeros to L.

The plaintext starts with 32 zero bytes, before the format byte. L and the file's size do not change; the room for records is 32 bytes less.

Every slot holds one seal count record: how many times the slot has been written, and when it was last written by the clock of the computer that wrote it.

A slot no passphrase opens is a nonce and L + 16 bytes, drawn when the vault is made from a ChaCha20 keystream keyed by HKDF-SHA256 of the computer's randomness: random-looking bytes the length of a used slot. The salt, the order in which the passphrases take the slots, and each used slot's first nonce come from the same keystream.

Opening

The reader checks the magic, the version, L against the four sizes, the file's length against L, and the cost against its limits: memory 8 MiB to 4 GiB, passes 1 to 10, lanes exactly 1. A file outside them is refused before any memory is taken. The memory a vault needs is in its header, and Unlock shows it before the passphrase is typed. Faraday makes vaults with at least 64 MiB and 2 passes.

The slot key is derived once and tried on all four slots. One tag verifies: that slot opens. None: the passphrase opens nothing. More than one: the file is refused, since Faraday never makes two slots under one key. Every slot is tried whichever opens, so the time taken does not show which slot a passphrase opens. A passphrase that opens none decrypts and reads no slot, which the result shows anyway. The opened slot's records are read, and any fault refuses the slot.

A slot whose tag verifies and whose first 32 bytes are not zero refuses the file.

Sealing

Sealing writes the open slot again under the same slot key with a new nonce, and copies the other three slots byte for byte. A session cannot tell an unused slot from one another passphrase opens, so it never rewrites one. The sealed vault is written to its stick as a new file, read back and compared byte for byte, and then takes the old copy's place.

Each seal's nonce is the first 24 bytes of a fresh answer from the system's randomness, when one is waiting. With none waiting, it is the first 24 bytes of SHA-256 over a label, the session's seed and a count of the nonces drawn that way, so a lock never waits on the system beyond its first answer.

Each seal writes the slot's seal count, one more than the last, and the time.

What a changed header can do

Changing the cost or the salt gives another key, so every slot fails. Changing L, the magic or the version fails the reader's checks, the length check or every tag. A slot moved to another position fails: its number is in its associated data. A slot moved to another vault fails: another salt gives another key, and another header is other associated data. The most a changed header can do is stop the vault opening, or, within the limits, make it slow or need more memory than a computer has.

What it promises

Without a passphrase, one copy of a vault shows four random-looking slots of equal size. It does not show how many passphrases exist or which slots are in use. Nothing in a slot can be read without its passphrase, and a changed byte makes its tag fail, so nothing in it can be changed unnoticed.

What it does not promise

Offline guessing. Anyone with the file has its salt and cost and can try passphrases on their own computers. Each guess costs one Argon2id at the vault's cost and tests all four slots. A slot is as strong as its passphrase: Passphrase strength, below.

Rollback. An older copy of a vault still opens. Someone who can write to the stick can put an older copy back; they cannot read or change anything in it.

Unlock shows a slot's seal count. An older copy is named when a newer one was opened, sealed or sat among the copies in Files since power-on, or when an open vault's backup plan records a newer one; that unlock stops at a warning naming it, and the vault opens only once the person presses Open. The count of a copy in Files is read with the key just opened, which the clean-process rule allows and a stick visit does not. A rollback is not seen when no newer copy is opened or read between power-on and the older copy's unlock and no open vault's plan records one, or when a vault and its plan are rolled back together.

A backup plan records, for each other vault it places, that vault's salt, size, slot position, seal count and a digest of the slot. Whoever opens the slot holding the plan learns those vaults' salts and sizes, which slot of each holds the plan's seeds, and how often it was sealed.

The seal's nonce. Two seals share a nonce only when no fresh answer from the system was waiting at either and the system gave both sessions the same seed, as a computer started again from a saved state, a virtual machine's snapshot for instance, can. Two seals under one key with one nonce show much of the slot to whoever has both copies, and let them forge it.

Two copies from different times. A slot's bytes change only when it is sealed. Someone with two copies sees which slots changed, and so which are in use. That weakens deniability, not secrecy.

Availability. A slot is checked only when it is opened. A changed byte in a slot no one opened is not noticed until its passphrase is next used, and then that slot is lost. A damaged header loses all four slots. A copy at another place is the remedy.

Key commitment. XChaCha20-Poly1305 does not commit to its key: a slot can be made whose tag verifies under two passphrases, showing other contents under each. Making one needs both passphrases.

The 32 zero bytes at the start of every slot's plaintext: under a second key they decrypt to other bytes, and the file is refused.

The seal count is read by whoever opens the slot. A decoy slot sealed twice beside one sealed daily says it is little used. Opening and sealing the decoy raises its count.

Link to this section

Passphrase strength

SpecificationOwn specificationCodeSpecification · 1 action

Words from dice

A passphrase of words drawn by dice from a list carries log2 of the list's length in bits for every word. Faraday has the three lists the EFF publishes (EFF word lists):

WordsEFF large list: 7 776 words, 12.9 bits a wordEFF short list 1: 1 296 words, 10.3 bits a wordEFF short list 2: 1 296 words, 10.3 bits a word
451.741.441.4
564.651.751.7
677.562.062.0
790.572.472.4
8103.482.782.7
9116.393.193.1
10129.2103.4103.4

The bits hold when every word is drawn by fair dice or by the computer's randomness, and not when a person chooses a word.

What Argon2id adds

Each guess costs one Argon2id at the vault's memory and passes, on the guesser's own hardware. Faraday states that work as log2(memory in KiB × passes) bits, which Create a vault shows beside each cost. The memory of the vaults Faraday makes ranges over a factor of 64, which is 6 bits; one more word from the large list is 12.9. The figure counts work, not how much more memory costs a guesser than time, and it gives no time per guess.

Other passphrases

A person may use any passphrase. Its strength is then theirs to ensure: Faraday does not measure a typed passphrase. A short passphrase, or one used anywhere else, is not protected by any cost.

Where to learn more

The EFF's page on dice-generated passphrases, eff.org/dice.

Arnold Reinhold's Diceware page, where the method began, theworld.com/~reinhold/diceware.html.

NIST Special Publication 800-63B, Digital Identity Guidelines, on what makes a password strong.

Link to this section

Make a vault

SpecificationRFC 9106 · RFC 5869 · draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha-03 · 2 morePublishedOwn specificationCodeLibrary: argon2 0.6Library: chacha20poly1305 0.11Library: chacha20 0.103 moreFaraday's code: faraday/faraday-vaultCross-checked withargon2-cffi (the Argon2 designers' reference code)Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Makes a vault file of four equal slots from one to four passphrases, with Argon2id, HKDF-SHA256 and XChaCha20-Poly1305, and a ChaCha20 keystream for the salt, the nonces and the unused slots.

Source

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-vault/src/lib.rs · create

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-vault/src/lib.rs · derive

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the argon2, hmac or chacha20poly1305 crates; the HKDF is two HMAC calls over secret input.

The Argon2id output, the HKDF keys and the slot key are Secret values wiped on drop; the Argon2id memory is overwritten before it is released; each passphrase's NFC form is wiped on drop (the normaliser's own buffer of combining marks is not); each slot's plaintext buffer is zeroized after it is encrypted.

Limits

Four slots, 1 to 4 passphrases, each non-empty and different from the others.

Slot sizes 64 KiB, 256 KiB, 1 MiB and 4 MiB only.

A vault is made at any cost the reader accepts: memory 8 MiB to 4 GiB, 1 to 10 passes, 1 lane. The app offers 64 MiB and 2 passes at least.

A cost whose memory cannot be allocated is an error that names the MiB needed.

A passphrase is put in Unicode NFC, and Argon2id takes that form's UTF-8 bytes; two passphrases with one NFC form are refused as the same.

Records given to be written may not hold a seal count (type 16): the vault writes it.

Faraday's own calls

Faraday's own container with four equal slots, so a file does not say how many slots are in use (docs/VAULT.md §9); unused slots are keystream, one salt serves every slot, and an older copy of a slot still opens. Kept until the outside review. Novel, F158.

The reader limits (lanes exactly 1, passes at most 10, memory 8 MiB to 4 GiB) refuse headers that would cost a guess or an allocation the vault does not state. Conservative, F155.

Every passphrase is put in Unicode NFC before Argon2id, and nothing else is done to it: compatibility characters, case and spaces are kept as typed (docs/VAULT.md §3). Neutral, F159.

Every slot's plaintext begins with 32 zero bytes, the padding fix for key commitment, so a slot that verifies under two keys shows them under at most one (docs/VAULT.md §4, §7). Conservative, F159.

Open a vault slot

SpecificationRFC 9106 · RFC 5869 · draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha-03 · 1 morePublishedOwn specificationCodeLibrary: chacha20poly1305 0.11Library: hmac 0.13Library: sha2 0.111 moreFaraday's code: faraday/faraday-vaultCross-checked withargon2-cffi (the Argon2 designers' reference code)Others' codeFaraday's second version
Action

Opens the slot a passphrase opens: checks the header, derives the slot key once with Argon2id and HKDF-SHA256, tries XChaCha20-Poly1305 on all four slots and parses the one whose tag verifies.

Specification

Own specification

RFC 9106, Argon2 Memory-Hard Function for Password Hashing and Proof-of-Work Applications, Informational

RFC 5869, HMAC-based Extract-and-Expand Key Derivation Function (HKDF), Informational

draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha-03, XChaCha: eXtended-nonce ChaCha and AEAD_XChaCha20_Poly1305, Draft

UAX #15, Unicode Standard Annex #15: Unicode Normalization Forms, Complete

Code

Library: chacha20poly1305 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: unicode-normalization 0.1.25, by the unicode-rs developers.

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-vault: The passphrase is put in NFC; all four slots are tried whichever opens, so the work does not say which slot a passphrase opens; a slot whose tag verifies and whose 32-byte commitment block is not zero, checked in constant time, refuses the file, as do two slots opening under one key; the slot's seal count is taken out of its records and kept with the open slot; another copy of the same vault (the same header) has its slot's seal count read with the key just opened, one decryption and no Argon2id, and a slot is told from another of the same count by its SHA-256; the header is checked before anything is allocated or derived.

Checked against

tools/vectors/vault

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-vault/src/lib.rs · open

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-keys/src/vault.rs · OpenVaults::unlock

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the chacha20poly1305 crate; the four tag checks are the same work whichever slot opens (faraday-vault's module documentation).

A slot's plaintext buffer is zeroized when its tag fails and when the contents have been parsed; the slot key is wiped when the vault closes.

Limits

Refuses a file whose magic, version, slot size, length or cost is not the format's, before any derivation.

Refuses a file in which more than one slot opens.

Refuses a file in which a slot's tag verifies and its first 32 bytes are not zero.

Refuses a slot without exactly one seal count, or with a count of 0.

A wrong passphrase and a file with no readable slot give the same refusal.

Faraday's own calls

Four tag checks on every open, so timing does not show which slot a passphrase opens. Conservative.

no decision

A slot whose tag verifies and whose commitment block is not zero refuses the whole file, as two slots opening under one key do: this format never writes one (docs/VAULT.md §4). Conservative, F159.

Specification

Own specification

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-vault/src/lib.rs · Opened::seal

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the chacha20poly1305 crate.

The slot's plaintext buffer is zeroized after it is encrypted.

Limits

Refuses a file whose header is not the one the slot was opened from.

Refuses records that do not fit the slot.

Refuses records that hold a seal count (type 16): the vault writes it.

Nothing is sealed before the session's seed has arrived.

The other three slots are never rewritten, re-randomised or cleared.

Faraday's own calls

The seal nonce is a fresh system answer when the pool holds one, and the session's seed with a count of seals only when none waits, so a lock never waits on the system (docs/VAULT.md §5). No two seals of one session share a nonce. Neutral, F158.

Each slot counts its seals (record 16): the count and the time by the sealing computer's clock, inside the slot and read by whoever opens it, so an older copy can be told from a newer one (docs/VAULT.md §5, §7). Neutral, F159.

Write a KeePass database

SpecificationKeePass KDBX 4 · RFC 9106 · RFC 8439PublishedCodeLibrary: argon2 0.6Library: chacha20 0.10Library: hmac 0.131 moreFaraday's code: core/faraday-backupCross-checked withpykeepassOthers' code
Action

Writes a KeePass KDBX 4.0 database of one to eight entries under a passphrase: Argon2id, SHA-256 and SHA-512 key steps, ChaCha20 over the body, HMAC-SHA-256 block authentication.

Specification

KeePass KDBX 4, The KeePass database format, version 4, Convention

RFC 9106, Argon2 Memory-Hard Function for Password Hashing and Proof-of-Work Applications, Informational

RFC 8439, ChaCha20 and Poly1305 for IETF Protocols, Status not checked

Code

Library: argon2 0.6.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: chacha20 0.10.2, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-backup: Write only: version 4.0, ChaCha20, no compression, the composite key is the passphrase alone; the check after writing reads the header hash, header HMAC and every block HMAC of a file this module wrote and parses no XML; the salt, seed, IV, inner key and UUIDs come from one caller-given seed. In the app that seed is the session's randomness, each 32 bytes from an answer of the system's of its own, and nothing is made until the system has answered.

Checked against

tools/vectors/backup

Faraday's own known answers.

tools/vectors/kdf

Published, by RFC 7914 §12, for scrypt; the Argon2id answer computed by argon2-cffi and PyCA cryptography, which agree.

core/faraday-backup/tests/vectors.rs::the_kdbx_vector_is_what_this_code_writes_and_reads_back_elsewhere

core/faraday-backup/src/kdbx.rs::a_written_database_verifies_under_its_passphrase

core/faraday-backup/src/kdbx.rs::one_changed_byte_anywhere_fails

core/faraday-backup/src/kdbx.rs::any_other_version_fails

core/faraday-backup/src/kdbx.rs::a_changed_cost_fails_before_any_key_derivation

faraday/faraday-core/tests/all/kdbx.rs::an_entry_exported_for_keepass_is_sealed_under_its_passphrase

no fuzz target: Nothing here reads a KDBX file but `verify`, which reads only a file this device wrote, and the tests' `open_for_tests`, which no shipped binary has

Cross-check

Compared with others' code: pykeepass, by the libkeepass project (the distribution's package).

Area kdbx

Faraday's code faraday_backup::kdbx (the KDBX 4 export and its own check)

Compared with pykeepass, the distribution's package (python3-pykeepass).

Checked first: Pykeepass is the distribution's package and the area stops and names it when it is missing, so a run without it fails; the versions are written into the stamp.

Vectors The repository's export (tools/vectors/backup/), written again byte for byte and opened.

Random cases Databases of one to eight items with markup characters, control characters, line ends and several scripts, at small Argon2id costs, every entry read back; the writer's check against pykeepass under the right passphrase, a wrong one and a changed byte.

Differences KDBX: pykeepass strips the characters XML 1.0 cannot hold from a protected value after decrypting it; Faraday writes a Password's bytes as they are. The area expects pykeepass's reading and counts the items that differ. pykeepass also opens a file whose major version is neither 3 nor 4 without reading past it, deriving a key or checking a hash; KeePass refuses such a file, and the area counts a file as opened only when pykeepass read a KDBX 4 body.

Pykeepass runs the key derivation the header states before it checks the header's SHA-256, so a changed bit in the Argon2 iterations (2^44 passes) holds it for good; KeePass and KeePassXC check the hash first and refuse at once. The area asks pykeepass first with a key given and nothing decrypted, which checks the version and the header's hash and derives nothing, and only then with the passphrase.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 120 cases

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the libraries; the check after writing compares the header hash, the header HMAC and the block HMACs with an ordinary comparison, on a file this device wrote.

The Argon2id output, the composite key, the encryption key and the HMAC base are Secret values wiped on drop; the document is built in a zeroizing buffer and encrypted in place; the Argon2id memory is overwritten before release.

Limits

1 to 8 entries, each field at most 16 KiB.

Version 4.0 only; nothing here reads a KDBX file made elsewhere.

A file whose version field is not 4.0, or whose cost was changed, fails the check after writing before any key is derived.

The export is as safe as its passphrase.

Faraday's own calls

Nostr entries are never exported (F27). Neutral, F27.

The file's seed, IV, salt, inner key and UUIDs are taken from the session's randomness, an answer for every 32 bytes. Neutral, F154.

RFC 6238, TOTP: Time-Based One-Time Password Algorithm, Informational

RFC 4226, HOTP: An HMAC-Based One-Time Password Algorithm, Informational

Code

Library: sha1 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-vault: Reads an otpauth://totp URI or a bare Base32 key; SHA1 by default as RFC 6238 does, SHA256 and SHA512 on request; the clock is the person's, checked or set by hand (docs/TOTP.md §1).

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-vault/src/totp.rs · Totp::code

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

Reveal::VaultEntry

Not stated by the hmac or sha1 crates.

The decoded secret is held in a zeroizing buffer; the HMAC output is a plain buffer dropped at the end of the call.

Limits

TOTP only: an otpauth://hotp URI is told apart and not computed.

Digits 6 to 8, period 1 to 3600 seconds; a parameter outside that is unreadable.

A bare Base32 key is read as SHA1, 6 digits, 30 seconds; a short key is refused.

No code is shown while the clock is not checked or set.

Faraday's own calls

A manual clock with a fixed-offset time zone, since a device with no network has no other time (F7). Neutral, F7.

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the ed25519-dalek crate.

The key's seeds are wiped when it drops; the kept revocation certificate is held in a zeroizing buffer while the record is made.

Limits

One key shape only: an Ed25519 primary that certifies and an Ed25519 subkey that signs.

A key's expiry is counted from its creation; 0 means never.

Faraday's own calls

Signing waits until the backup flow has written every place the key's plan gives it, two or more; GnuPG has no such gate. Conservative, F32.

The primary key's flags are certify only and the signature hash is SHA-512, as GnuPG 2.4 chooses for Ed25519. Neutral, F32.

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

Reveal::OutboxFile

Not stated by the ed25519-dalek crate.

The primary seed is wiped when the key value drops; a kept revocation is a record field of the open vault, wiped when it closes.

Limits

Reasons are GnuPG's menu: none, compromised, superseded, no longer used.

The kept revocation is not replaced when a new one is made.

The file is a secret file: it goes through the secret sheet, with no code or picture.

Faraday's own calls

The revocation is kept in the key's vault record and nothing is written to the Outbox at key generation. Neutral, F32.

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-pgp/src/certificate.rs · restore

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-keys/src/gpg.rs · Restore::finish

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the ed25519-dalek crate.

The typed lines, a scanned code's lines and the restored seeds are wiped when the restore is wiped or dropped; a code refused keeps nothing.

Limits

Refuses a paperkey protected by a passphrase, one of another algorithm, one for another key or subkey, and one whose secret does not make the public key.

A certification by another key is set aside and counted, not restored.

The public key is read from Files, a scanned one filed there first; a paperkey's code is taken whole or not at all, every line checked as typed, the raw form by its whole CRC.

Faraday's own calls

A restore that cannot reproduce the certificate's own signatures is refused rather than accepted on the public key alone. Conservative, F155.

Link to this action

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-pgp/src/verify.rs · verify_detached

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not applicable: only public keys and signatures are handled.

Nothing secret is held.

Limits

Refuses text-mode signatures, versions 5 and 6, a hashed signature expiry, a creation time outside the hashed area and a critical subpacket it does not understand.

Refuses a certificate that carries a revocation, and a signature made before the subkey or after either key's expiry.

Armour without its checksum, or with anything after it, is refused.

Faraday's own calls

Anything not honoured is refused rather than read, so the release check of F134 cannot be satisfied by a signature of another shape. Conservative, F134.

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-sb/src/lib.rs · make

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-sb/src/lib.rs · enrolment

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the rsa crate.

The keys are returned to the caller, which keeps them in the vault's type 8 record; the seed is the caller's.

Limits

RSA-2048 only, certificates valid 30 years.

Faraday never writes an EFI variable: enrolment happens in the firmware's own setup screen.

The Windows policy adds Microsoft's KEK and Windows signing CAs, not the third-party CA.

Faraday's own calls

An RNG adapter written here, ChaCha20 (RFC 8439) as a stream keyed by a fresh 32-byte draw with the purpose in the nonce's first byte, feeds a pre-1.0 release of the rsa crate; its keystream is checked against RFC 8439 and every key it makes by openssl pkey -check. Novel, F158.

0.1.9 · faraday/faraday-sb/src/release.rs · sign

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Not stated by the rsa crate.

The db key is the vault record's, held by the open vault and wiped when it closes; the blinding stream is a ChaCha20 keystream of fresh bytes from the system.

Limits

Signs only a release's own boot program, and not an older release's: the release must be at least as new as the running Faraday.

Refuses a file the sums do not list exactly once, sums whose first line does not name the release, and a text-mode signature.

The signature is appended as one certificate table at the image's end.

Faraday's own calls

A boot program is signed only when it is a release's own. Conservative, F134.

Runs eight fixed-vector checks at start-up and from Settings: BIP 39 words and seed in English and Japanese, BIP 32 derivation, a BIP 84 address, an ECDSA and a BIP 340 signature, scrypt and Argon2id.

Write an image to a stick

SpecificationFIPS 180-4PublishedCodeLibrary: sha2 0.11Library: lzma-rust2 0.22Library: getrandom 0.3 and 0.4Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-payloadCross-checked withNot cross-checked
Action

Writes an image to a stick and checks it: the SHA-256 of the payload file against its manifest before any write, of the decompressed bytes against the manifest as they are written, and of what was read back from the stick, with the stick's own IDs and name taken back out, against the manifest after.

Specification

FIPS 180-4, Secure Hash Standard, Status not checked

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: lzma-rust2 0.22.0, by hasenbanck (lzma-rust2), a port of the Tukaani project's XZ for Java.

Library: getrandom 0.3.4 and 0.4.3, by the Rand Project developers.

Faraday's code: faraday/faraday-payload: The payload is read whole and its SHA-256 compared before anything is written, then read again and decompressed into the writer; success means the writer received exactly the manifest's size with the manifest's SHA-256; a developer's image is hashed when confirmed and refused if it changed after; a whole stick is read back and compared with the stream as written, and the SHA-256 of what was read, with the image's own IDs put back where the stick's went (docs/STICK-IDENTITY.md §4.3), must be the manifest's. The stick's own GUIDs and FAT serials come from getrandom; they are identities, not secrets, and the GPT's CRC-32s are worked out again over them. A name given the data partition (docs/STICK-IDENTITY.md §6) is faraday-fat's label write run over the sectors captured, each byte it changed a patch put back the same way.

Checked against

not checked against vectors

faraday/faraday-payload/tests/payload.rs::a_payload_made_by_xz_decodes_to_exactly_the_original_bytes

faraday/faraday-payload/tests/payload.rs::a_payload_whose_xz_sum_does_not_match_is_refused_before_anything_is_written

faraday/faraday-payload/tests/payload.rs::decompressed_bytes_that_do_not_match_the_manifest_are_a_failure

faraday/faraday-payload/tests/payload.rs::a_developers_image_has_the_sum_sha256sum_gives_plain_or_xz

faraday/faraday-payload/tests/payload.rs::a_developers_image_that_changed_after_it_was_confirmed_is_a_failure

faraday/shells/starter/tests/all/ids.rs::two_sticks_written_from_one_image_have_their_own_ids_and_each_checks_against_the_manifest

no fuzz target: The manifest target fuzzes the manifest's reading; no target runs the xz decoder or the hash

Cross-check

not cross-checked: The sums are compared with `sha256sum`'s in the tests; no F79 area runs the decoder

Limits

Hash and authenticate bytes

SpecificationFIPS 180-4 · RFC 2104 · RFC 4231 · 3 morePublishedCodeLibrary: sha2 0.11Library: hmac 0.13Library: pbkdf2 0.13Faraday's code: core/faraday-cryptoCross-checked withpython-mnemonicOthers' code
Action

Hashes and authenticates bytes with SHA-256, HMAC-SHA256 and HMAC-SHA512, expands a key with HKDF-Expand over SHA-256, and stretches a password with PBKDF2.

Specification

FIPS 180-4, Secure Hash Standard, Status not checked

RFC 2104, HMAC: Keyed-Hashing for Message Authentication, Status not checked

RFC 4231, Identifiers and Test Vectors for HMAC-SHA-224, HMAC-SHA-256, HMAC-SHA-384, and HMAC-SHA-512, Status not checked

RFC 5869, HMAC-based Extract-and-Expand Key Derivation Function (HKDF), Informational

RFC 8018, PKCS #5: Password-Based Cryptography Specification Version 2.1, Status not checked

RFC 7914, The scrypt Password-Based Key Derivation Function, Status not checked

Code

Library: sha2 0.11.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: hmac 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Library: pbkdf2 0.13.0, by the RustCrypto developers.

Faraday's code: core/faraday-crypto: Thin wrappers, the one entry point the rest of the core uses, so the choice of crate is confined here; any HMAC key length is accepted; the whole-file SHA-256 and the key-stretching calls count themselves on the compute token. HKDF-Expand (RFC 5869 §2.3) is written here over `hmac`, for the blinding of the session's keys and Schnorr signing's per-input auxiliary randomness; it is checked against RFC 5869's test cases 1 to 3. The bip39 area reaches SHA-256 and PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 only; HMAC-SHA256, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 and HKDF-Expand are checked by their RFCs' cases here.

Area sps

Faraday's code faraday_psbt::silent, faraday_bip::dleq

Compared with BIP-374's reference.py (bip374/) and BIP-375's validator and test_runner.py (bip375/), over secp256k1lab.

Checked first: Its vendored files are hashed against references.sha256 before any run, the validator is required to give each of BIP-375's 43 vectors its published verdict and the reference to give each of BIP-374's rows its published answer.

Vectors Both CSV files of tools/vectors/bip374/ and tools/vectors/bip375/bip375_test_vectors.json.

Random cases Proofs made and verified, damaged or not, under two generators; version 2 PSBTs of one to four inputs of every kind BIP-375 reads and some it does not, one to three silent payment outputs, shares added by one signer or two, a global share, and each finished PSBT damaged in one of eighteen ways, judged by the BIP's validator.

Differences BIP-375: Faraday takes a key a PSBT states for a P2PKH, P2SH-P2WPKH or P2WPKH input only where the input's script commits to it (F89); the validator takes the first key stated, so a PSBT it accepts with an unbound key is refused by Faraday.

BIP-375: Faraday accepts the scripts of a scan key's outputs numbered in sorted order of their spend keys, as the BIP's text says, and in output order, as its vectors do; the validator accepts output order only.

BIP-375: Faraday refuses an input whose redeem script is not the one its P2SH script hashes, and one whose previous transaction is not the one spent; the validator reads both fields without checking them.

When it runs Whenever the crypto changes, and with a new seed and about ten times the cases before every release.

Last passing run Seed faraday-crosscheck-1 · 80 cases

Source

0.1.9 · core/faraday-psbt/src/silent.rs · add_shares

0.1.9 · core/faraday-bip/src/dleq.rs · generate_proof

github.com/bitcoinpasada/faraday

Secret handling

none: no secret leaves faraday-keys

Every secret multiplication goes through secp256k1's ECDH, documented in the code as taking the same time for every scalar; the public challenge and response use a multiplication the code says is right for public values only.

The secret's bytes, the nonce and the hashes they pass through are zeroized before return; each private key used is erased before its input is done.

Limits

Built in core and not reachable from the app: the device does not send.

An eligible input whose public key the PSBT does not state, a P2SH input with no redeem script and a previous transaction that is not the one spent stop the check.

A SegWit version above 1 and a sighash other than ALL are refused.

Faraday's own calls

A key a PSBT states for an input counts only where the input's script commits to it, which is stricter than BIP 375's reference validator. Conservative, F89.

Spends of SegWit above v1 and sighashes other than ALL are refused. Conservative.

no decision
